**How to read this document.** §0 is the summary: decisions, guarantees, deviations, non-goals. §1 fixes scope, requirements and the exit gate; §2 the threat and privacy model of the distribution chain. §3–§8 are normative: keys and ceremonies (§3), data formats at byte level (§4), the transparency log and attestations (§5), the reproducible build (§6), the update protocol and the client (§7), the channels (§8). §9 is the integration (modules, JNI, `:sync`, schema), §10 the invariants, §11 tests and the exit-gate argument, §12 gates, CI and dependencies, §13 slices, effort and the checklist, §14 risks, §15 owner questions, §16 owner actions and legal items, §17 findings outside the phase, §18 the first design review's findings and what became of each. Appendix A holds the ADR drafts (Romanian), Appendix B the text changes in other documents (Romanian where the target is Romanian), Appendix C the evidence and the S0 checks, Appendix D the sources. A section marked "(normative)" wins over a summary table.

**Identifier conventions.** Decisions `D1…D26`; requirements `RQ-D1…RQ-D33`; guarantees `CS-D1…` (security and correctness) and `P-D1…P-D5` (privacy); deviations `X-D1…X-D17`; threat scenarios `DS-1…DS-22`; keys `K-…`; ceremonies `RK0…RK6`; formats `GHRT` (root), `GHRC` (root chain), `GHRM` (manifest), `GHRA` (attestation statement), `GHRP` (log proof), `GHRB` (bundle); invariants `T48…T52`; gates `G-D1…G-D11`; mutants `MC-D1…MC-D32` (security, correctness) and `PM-D1…PM-D13` (privacy); twin worlds `NI-U1…NI-U5`; S0 checks `V-D1…V-D26` (V-D11 split into V-D11a, V-D11b); risks `R-D1…R-D21`; questions `Q1…Q49`; owner actions `OA-1…OA-13`; legal items `LG-1…`; findings `F-D1…F-D12`; residues `L10-D1…L10-D25` (new LIMITE section L10); review findings by the reviewers' own ids (§18). Slices `S0a`, `S0b`, `S1…S10` with `S5a` and `S6b`, and the deferred `S-store`.

## 0. Summary

### 0.1 Decisions

| # | Decision | Rejected alternatives (why) | Plan / ADR alignment |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | **Channels.** The onion mirror is the primary channel and the only one built in Phase 14: static, signed, logged files on ≥ 2 onion services. First installs come from the mirror (Tor Browser, through each mirror's HTTP/1.1 listener: browsers do not speak HTTP/2 without TLS, §7.1) or any channel at all, checked by the standalone verifier (D21). **Accrescent** is deferred behind conditions GHOST does not control today (sign-up open only to allowlisted accounts, verification of the app-ID domain, no self-updater in the uploaded app, APK sets only; §8.2). **No F-Droid-format repository** (Q71's default, applied under the standing instruction, `faza9-s0-hygiene.md:147`). | Google Play (Google holds the app signing key, `support.google.com/…/9842756`; legal identity of the developer; A7, TB-6); the F-Droid main repository (FLOSS only, inclusion policy); a clearnet HTTPS mirror (users' IPs and a GHOST domain on clearnet, against LIM:158); IPFS (gateway sees IP and version; removed by ADR-07); building the Accrescent flavor now (no admission, cost ≈ 1 week, Q26) | FR-8.10 (plan:279), ADR-37 point 7 (`ADR-37:20`), Q71; deviation X-D6 |
| D2 | **Networks and identities.** Stagenet and mainnet each get their own trust root and their **own Android signing key** (spec §12.1, "separate signing and secrets", p.21); stagenet builds carry `applicationIdSuffix ".stagenet"`, so an alpha install can never be updated to, or by, a production APK. The final `applicationId` is decided before the first distribution (Q3): it is permanent after one release, and `org.ghost.app` (`app/build.gradle.kts:11`) names a domain the project does not hold. | One signing key for both networks (spec §12.1; an alpha key compromise would reach production installs); keeping `org.ghost.app` by inertia (Accrescent's domain check, §8.2; a name already used by others needs extra review under Android developer verification, §8.5) | spec §12.1; ADR-22 point 4 (keys pinned per network) |
| D3 | **Roles and algorithms** (§3.1). `K-APK`: the Android signing identity, **ECDSA P-256** (APK v2 algorithm 0x0201; Ed25519 is not an APK signature algorithm). `K-REL1`, `K-REL2`: release keys, **Ed25519**, threshold 1 of 2. `K-ROOT1…3`: root keys, Ed25519, threshold 2 of 3, one held by a second party; a root key may also sign the log leaf of the next root (§4.3), so losing both release tokens never blocks a root update. `K-ATT-*`: independent builders' (auditors') attestation keys, Ed25519, listed in the root. The schedule key `K-SCHED` keeps its role (ES). Every key is listed in a versioned root document; losing or rotating a release, attestation, log or witness key is a root update, not an app update. | Ed25519 for the APK (impossible); RSA-4096 for the APK (valid, slower on tokens, no gain; RSA-3072 is not in the v2 size list; recorded alternative); one pinned manifest key without a root (its loss strands every installed updater); FROST or multi-party signatures (no token support; independent signatures with a threshold give the same property) | A7, AS-10 (`TM:25,41`), FR-8.2 |
| D4 | **Custody** (§3.2, §3.3). Hardware tokens: YubiKey 5 series with firmware ≥ 5.7 (PIV Ed25519), **six per network**, one role per token: T1, T2 `K-APK`; T5, T6 the release keys (never on a `K-APK` token, so one stolen token and its PIN never yield both an installable APK and a manifest, Q48); T3 `K-ROOT1`; T4 `K-ROOT3` (second party). `K-APK` is generated on the air-gapped signer host, imported into two tokens and split by **SLIP-39 3-of-5** into paper shares in separate custody, because a lost Android signing key cannot be rotated away (rotation needs the old key's signature) and is a new app identity. Release keys are generated on their tokens (loss → root update, which a root key can log). `K-ROOT2` is SLIP-39 2-of-3 shares at three custodians, the owner holding at most one. The offline signer host is a dedicated air-gapped laptop booted from a verified read-only image. Stagenet may use the simplified custody of Q43 (declared). | On-token generation of `K-APK` (no backup: loss = every user reinstalls and loses local data); cloud KMS (online, identity-bound, clearnet); YubiHSM 2 (sound, ≈ 10× the cost; alternative in Q6); a software key on the online workstation (TB-6, A7) | FR-8.2 "offline/HSM-backed storage, multi-party access, documented rotation"; R-11 (spec p.25) |
| D5 | **Ceremonies** (§3.4): RK0 genesis (two people on mainnet; the owner with a recorded checklist on stagenet, Q7), RK1 release and heartbeat signing (the owner alone; B's sums confirmed on an independent device), RK2 root update (root threshold, a second party; no release token needed), RK3 Android-key rotation (on compromise only: afterwards every release is signed by the original and the new key while `minSdk` < 33, §6.5, Q9), RK4 compromise response (including recovery after a 2-of-3 root compromise, §3.5), RK5 annual restore drill, RK6 heartbeat-only re-signing (at least every 77 days). **Multi-party** applies to the root and to recovery, **not** to each release (deviation X-D5): a solo owner must be able to ship a security fix alone. | Two people for every release (unoperable for a solo owner); one person for everything (no recovery, no check on root changes) | FR-8.2; K1 precedent (`faza8-issuer.md:456`) |
| D6 | **Short signed statements** (§4.2): every GHOST signature is Ed25519 (`verify_strict`) over `"ghost/v1/<type>" ‖ 0x00 ‖ network ‖ SHA-256(body)` (≤ 60 bytes), so a hardware token signs it whatever the body's size. | Signing whole bodies as the ES does (`schedule.rs:282-284`): tokens reportedly cap Ed25519 messages near 1 KiB (V-D7); JSON/JWS (parsing surface; the ES precedent is canonical binary) | CP-09 exception recorded in ADR-49 with vectors |
| D7 | **Formats** (§4): canonical binary documents in the ES style — root `GHRT` (versioned, N→N+1 needs both thresholds), root chain `GHRC`, manifest `GHRM`, attestation statement `GHRA`, log proof `GHRP`, bundle `GHRB` — with TUF's properties (thresholds, versioned root, expiry of the manifest, monotonic sequence, exact hash and length, size bounds) and TUF's attack list as tests. The root carries the **anchor mirrors** (§4.3) and no expiry: TUF's root-expiry freeze check (§5.3.10) is met by the manifest's expiry, because every offer needs an unexpired manifest signed under the trusted root and every update run asks at least one root-listed mirror (§7.8). | TUF through `tough` 0.24.0 (pulls `aws-lc-rs`, C and assembly, into the APK; JSON parsing; no delegations; a timestamp role that wants an online key, TB-6); python-tuf / go-tuf (not Rust, not Android); in-toto layouts (no client) | spec §12.2 steps 1–4 |
| D8 | **Freshness**: a manifest expires at most **91 days** after issue (`not_after_day`) and is re-signed (RK6) or replaced by a release at least **14 days before** it expires (by day 77), which covers a device clock up to 3 days fast and the update-run latency of §7.3; every update run reads the bundle from **two mirrors, at least one listed by the trusted root** (§7.8), and the highest `(root_version, manifest_seq)` pair wins; time checks tolerate σ + 1 day (σ = 3 days, F7:58); an expired manifest makes the channel STALE (no offers, no directory or mirror change; the app keeps working). | An online timestamp key (TB-6, A7); freshness from witness timestamps with a scan of the log's tail (a shared public log's tail is large over Tor); no expiry (unbounded freeze by stale mirrors); 6 weeks (stronger bound, monthly ceremonies: Q8) | spec §12.2 step 1 ("at least two independent locations"); TM S10 (`TM:165`) |
| D9 | **Binary transparency log: Sigsum** (log protocol v1, stable), public logs with a named trust policy (2 logs; witnesses Glasklar Teknik, Mullvad VPN, Tillitis). Each manifest is logged as a Sigsum leaf (`message = SHA-256(manifest)`) signed by a release key, each new root by a root or release key of the previous root; mirrors serve the inclusion proof and the cosigned tree head; **clients never contact the log** and verify offline; cosignatures need no freshness, so a dead log never strands clients. | Rekor v2 (witnessing "will be implemented soon" at GA; yearly log shards); a GHOST-run log (its operator is the vendor, witnesses would need recruiting); no log (ADR-38 point 6, approved) | ADR-38 point 6 (`ADR-38:19`); spec §12.2 step 2 "release-registry commitment" (X-D3) |
| D10 | **Attestation-bound updates** (§5.5, §7.4): the updater parses the downloaded APK as Android does (EOCD, central-directory offset, APK Signing Block before it, and the signer the platform uses at the device's SDK: v3.1, else v3, else v2) and hashes its **unsigned core** — the file without the block and without the zero padding apksigner writes before it, with the end record pointing where the unsigned central directory began (§6.5, measured); the core must equal the release's attested unsigned build, signed by at least the root's attestation threshold of independent builders (0 on stagenet until an auditor exists; ≥ 1 on mainnet). T12b becomes a property the installed app enforces. | Trusting the release key's word for the build (AD-V signs anything); attestations from GitHub's hosted runner (no keys in CI, TB-6; GitHub artifact attestations need GitHub Enterprise Cloud for a private repository, `actions/attest-build-provenance` README) | ADR-38 point 5 (T12b), FR-8.9 (plan:278) |
| D11 | **Builder and two environments** (§6): one recipe (`infra/builder/Dockerfile` + `pins.txt`), every input pinned by digest or SHA-256 (the SDK, NDK and Rust archives included, fetched directly rather than through `sdkmanager` or `rustup`), built inside the container from a git checkout of a signed tag (the sources on tmpfs), at fixed paths, with the processor count every tool sees pinned to 2 (`-XX:ActiveProcessorCount`, `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS`, Gradle workers; not `docker run --cpus`, which Docker refuses above the host's CPU count). Environment A: the owner's workstation (Docker). Environment B: a GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-24.04` runner (2 CPUs, 8 GB) within a written minute budget (Q42). `SHA256SUMS(A) = SHA256SUMS(B)` before signing, B's sums confirmed on an independent device. Environment C, the auditor's, is a release gate (FR-8.9). | Native Windows builds (backslash paths, `build-native.sh:9-11`); Cargo `trim-paths` (open PR #17488, targeted at Rust 1.101; the pin is 1.98.1); Nix (+1–2 weeks to learn, still Google binaries); Guix (no Android SDK); a prebuilt image alone (not itself reproducible); the self-hosted runner as B (same laptop and WSL2 VM as A, `ci-self-hosted.md` §7) | plan:318 gate; INV T12 (`INV:20`); ADR-19 (`ADR-19:13`) |
| D12 | **Release artifact set** (§6.1): the zip-aligned unsigned APK; both stripped native libraries **and their split symbol files** (line tables, from a dedicated `client-release` profile, so operator binaries stay stripped), bound by a deterministic GNU build-id and by the manifest; `ghost-relay`, `ghost-issuer`, `ghost-issuer-ops`, `ghost-release-ops`, each built in its own `cargo` invocation; `ghost-verify` for Linux and Windows with the exported public source; the notices; CycloneDX 1.7 SBOMs generated deterministically; AGP's `vcsInfo` **off** (the commit is bound by the logged manifest and the attestations, so a source-escrow tarball also reproduces). | Shipping unstripped libraries (size, NFR-4); symbols without a build-id (no standard symbolication); keeping `vcsInfo` (builds from a tarball differ; the file adds nothing the log lacks); SPDX 2.3 (requires a creation timestamp) | ADR-38 points 5, 9 (`ADR-38:18,22`); ADR-37 point 8; C-08 |
| D13 | **Signing preserves the core** (§6.5): `apksigner` from build-tools 37.0.0 with `--alignment-preserved true`, v2 and v3 on, v1 and v4 off (after RK3: the original and the new key in one run, `--next-signer` and `--lineage`); `ghost-release-ops` checks `core(signed) = unsigned` byte for byte under §6.5's padding rule before anything is logged or published. | `apksigcopier` (archived 2025-05-12, GPLv3+); the default re-alignment of build-tools ≥ 35 (rewrites padding, breaks the relation; F-Droid issue 3299); revision 1's "signed = unsigned plus the block" (false: apksigner inserts zero padding before the block, §6.5) | — |
| D14 | **Mirrors and protocol** (§7.1, §7.2): static files over **HTTP/2 with prior knowledge** inside the onion stream for the app (the client's existing hyper `http2` stack, its SETTINGS pinned and golden-tested), and an **HTTP/1.1 listener on port 80 of the same onion** for browsers and `ghost-verify`; fixed paths, one bundle per network, APKs by SHA-256 with `Range` in fixed 4 MiB steps, paths and ranges built natively, no client-specific header, no logs (a configuration gate and a container test, G-D10), PoW defenses kept (GHOST clients do not solve PoW: declared, Q40). Hosting never on the owner's home connection, the signer or a CI host. Server: nginx if S0 confirms both listeners (V-D12, V-D22), else a ~300-line `ghost-mirror`. | HTTP/1.1 in the app (a second HTTP parser in the client); h2c only (Tor Browser cannot fetch, so first installs fail); gRPC (third parties could not mirror plain files); relays as mirrors (capabilities, circuit linkage) | ADR-01 (`ADR-01:14`), T6 |
| D15 | **Update runs** (§7.3): a third kind of periodic run, drawn from client randomness only (1 in 24 of the runs that are not quiet runs; precedence RETURN, quiet, update, relay session; not held by the payment hold), with neither a relay session nor an issuer call; its own isolation scope `Update`; two mirrors; a **fixed duration** whether or not a download is pending, so an offer never changes a job's length or the JobScheduler quota left for later jobs; the APK is downloaded in update runs (resumable) or at the user's request (foreground; it may overlap a foreground relay session, declared, Q47). **No check at app start** (deviation X-D1 from FR-7.5's "startup update checks"). | A check at app open (the mirror would see app opens: T19's spirit, ADR-15); inside quiet runs (co-occurs with issuer calls, T23); during background relay sessions; downloading "until the lease deadline" (revision 1: a pending download lengthened the job and delayed later relay sessions, which relays could see) | FR-7.5; T19, T23 (`INV:27,31`) |
| D16 | **Anti-rollback** (§7.5): the platform's `versionCode` rule plus persisted, append-only facts within a **trust epoch** (root chain, the `(root_version, manifest_seq)` pair and its hash, the floor scoped to its root version and bounded by the highest attested release of its manifest); an equal pair with other bytes = `CONFLICT`, kept until a newer root; a new root version starts a new sequence space (escape from a fast-forwarded sequence) and a new floor scope (escape from a fast-forwarded floor); the **trust anchor** is the installed APK's embedded root unless the stored root is on its chain, so a recovery APK starts a new epoch (§7.4 step 0); `versionCode` = a release counter in a committed file, checked against the log by the release tool. No downgrade path (deviation X-D4). | An "emergency recovery" downgrade (Android refuses lower `versionCode`; uninstall loses local data); revision 1's anchor "the newer root by version" (a recovery root never wins, and an attacker holding two root keys outruns it); an unbounded, unscoped floor (one stolen release key ends every offer for good) | CP-08, FR-7.5, spec §12.2 step 3, C-07 |
| D17 | **Staged rollout and halt** (§7.6): a per-release permille and salt in the manifest, the salt fixed for the release's life; the bucket is `HMAC(rollout_key, "ghost/v1/rollout" ‖ apk_sha256 ‖ salt)` from a per-install secret that never leaves the device; halt = permille 0; **rollback = roll-forward** (the previous source with a higher `versionCode`), since a schema version and an ES never go back. **Wire rule (P-D5):** a release that changes anything relays or the issuer observe ships the change dormant and activates it for everyone at one ES week (Q46), so a staged rollout never makes the updated few percent a cohort relays can see. | Server-side cohorts (identifiers); one fixed bucket per install (the same users always first); a salt the vendor may reuse across releases (a reused salt recreated the same first cohort); staging wire changes (the 5 % cohort is visible to every relay) | FR-8.8, spec §12.2 step 5, C-12, §13.1 "missing release rollback" |
| D18 | **Installer hand-off** (§7.7): a `PackageInstaller` session, the user always confirms; the verified bytes are streamed into the session through the native verifier (no check-then-use window); `REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES` joins T15 by ADR-51; no silent updates. | `ACTION_VIEW` on a file URI (a `FileProvider`, a second copy); `UPDATE_PACKAGES_WITHOUT_USER_ACTION` (user control; Phase 13 may revisit, Q16) | T15 (`common.sh:15`), `AndroidManifest.xml:5` |
| D19 | **Directory, mirrors and ES** (§7.8, §7.9): bootstrap relay entries compiled into the native library from the committed, gate-checked `relay-directory.txt` (source `config`; an early slice, S5a, since no production code fills the directory today); **anchor mirrors in the root** (root threshold), the manifest only **adding** mirrors; the first fresh verified manifest takes the relay directory over and later fresh ones add and withdraw relays (bootstrap entries included), so a hostile relay leaves every client without an app release; **operator ids are write-once per onion** (no manifest can relabel the operators that relay sets count, F9 §5.2); routine relay changes travel with the ES's week schedule, a manifest withdrawing a relay of a current or near ES week only as a named emergency; the ES stays embedded (no network path, ADR-22 point 5) and each release records `es_seq` and `es_sha256`, so a changed ES is visible in the log (F8 F6). | ES over the network (ADR-22); the release key signing the ES (GHES v1 signs its whole body, so a token cannot sign it, F-D1); no bootstrap (production code fills the directory nowhere today, F-D3) | `RelayDirectory.kt:5`, `relay-directory.txt:2-3`, `ADR-22:23,45`, `faza8-issuer.md:2255` |
| D20 | **Module split** (§9): a new Rust crate `ghost-client-update` (pure: no I/O, clock or logging) in `libghost_client_net.so`; the mirror fetch in `client-core/net` on `IsolationScope::Update`; a **closed JNI surface** `org.ghost.network.UpdateNative` and `TorMirrorTransport` (gate G-D5, implemented as an extension of G-R6's lists; the native side builds every mirror path and range from verified state); a Kotlin module `:update`; update runs in `:sync`; schema migration U; the offline tool `ghost-release-ops`; the public verifier `ghost-verify` on the same format crate. No new external crate in any shipped graph (one new allowlist entry: the workspace crate `ghost-client-update` itself); the Rust mutants behind a `mutants` feature only the host test library enables. | Verification in Kotlin (ADR-19); a second native library (a second Rust runtime) | ADR-19 (`ADR-19:13`), ADR-20 |
| D21 | **First install, operators, monitors** (§5.4): the standalone verifier `ghost-verify` (desktop CLI for Linux and Windows, built by the release recipe and published with its source in the public repository and in the mirrors' archive; offline proof checks; optional Tor fetch) and the signing-certificate fingerprint published in two independent places (the public repository, reachable over Tor, and the auditor's attestation) besides the mirrors (apksigner, AppVerifier); relay operators check `ghost-relay` against the logged release before running it, and operator images take the released binary; a public monitor, run as a release gate by an independent party (the auditor) as well as by the owner, checks every logged GHOST leaf against the archive and against what the mirrors actually served. | An in-app self-check (a malicious APK would skip it; the facade exposes no "verify this app" value); trusting store or mirror operators; a monitor run only by the owner (it cannot detect the owner) | ADR-38 points 6, 7 (`ADR-38:19-20`), F9 §22.8.8 point 3 |
| D22 | **Provenance and publication** (§5.3): releases only from **signed tags** (hardware-backed key, committed allowed-signers file); the release record (manifests, proofs, APKs, symbols, SBOMs, notices, MPL sources, ES files, relay binary, `ghost-verify`, attestations, escrow digest, the per-version evidence of ADR-38 point 8) is published in the mirrors' archive and referenced by hash from the logged manifest; the publication runs in a container whose only network is Tor (§5.2). | Publishing from CI or on GitHub (G-L5, `no-public-binaries.sh:22-24`; ADR-39); CI run links in the record (the repository is private, so they verify nothing and name the owner's account); `torsocks` around Go tools (Go makes raw syscalls, so traffic would leave outside Tor) | FR-8.1 (STATUS:25 gap), C-08; ADR-38 point 8 |
| D23 | **Supply-chain hardening in scope**: pin the `aapt2` jars (today trusted without checksum, `verification-metadata.xml:8`), pin the JDK patch level, one pins file feeding the builder and CI (G-D2); Gradle signature verification decided in S0 (Q22); `cargo-vet` not adopted (Q23). | — | T7, AD-9 (`TM:58`) |
| D24 | **Android developer verification and Play Protect** are platform facts outside GHOST's control: no registration in Phase 14; the owner decides before Phase 16 onboards alpha testers, with counsel (Q18); the Android key's backup keeps registration possible; the residues are declared (L10-D9, L10-D10, and the verifier's install and update lookups to Google, L10-D24). | Registering now (ties the owner's legal identity to the package at Google before it is needed); the limited-distribution account for the alpha (≤ 20 devices, each registered with Google by a code generated on it) | §8.5 |
| D25 | **Identity of public material** (ADR-39 points 4 and 6): everything published — the public repository (a new one, ADR-38 point 7), the APK's notices, SBOMs, symbol files, the release record, the RK1 checklist, the domain and the Sigsum submissions — carries a **project identity** (a provisional project name and accounts from S3 on; the legal holder of the copyright line decided with counsel before Phase 16 onboards testers, Q45), never the owner's personal name, account, email or paths; gate G-D9 scans every release output for personal identifiers. Today the notices preamble names the owner (`licenses/apk-notices-preamble.txt:4`) and `ghost/Cargo.toml:34` names the personal GitHub account, which `cargo metadata` carries into an SBOM. | Publishing under the personal identity (contrary to ADR-39 point 4; irreversible once an APK ships); deciding after the first distribution | ADR-39 points 4, 6; ADR-38 point 7; LG-3, LG-4 |
| D26 | **Live-drill harness** (§11.4; Q37): the updater is driven on a real phone by the `gate` build type's instrumentation (the Phase 9 pattern), with a **drill root** of its own (stagenet network byte, software keys, never in a stagenet release) whose bundles the two real staging mirrors serve and the real log records; the RK1-signed stagenet releases are installed on the same phone with `adb install -r` for the platform's half (signature continuity, lineage, downgrade refusal). A release build has no UI, no logging and no exported trigger, and an instrumentation APK must carry the target's signature. | Signing an instrumentation APK with the stagenet `K-APK` (it could run code inside any stagenet install over adb, which Phase 16's alpha testers carry); a trigger in the release build (T15m, G-D1) | plan:318 gate; F9 §15.10 |

### 0.2 Guarantees claimed (each has a test in §11; none is a UI claim before the gate closes, CP-10)

| ID | Guarantee | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| CS-D1 | **Signed and logged.** The updater installs only APK bytes listed in a manifest signed by the release threshold of its **trusted root** (the newest root reachable by the chain rule from its trust anchor, §7.4 steps 0 and 2), and only when that manifest is included in a policy log under a checkpoint cosigned by the witness quorum. | T48: vectors, tamper suites (§11.1), MC-D1 … MC-D12, MC-D20, MC-D23, MC-D29 |
| CS-D2 | **Reproduced before trusted** (when the root's attestation threshold is ≥ 1). The unsigned core of the installed APK, found as Android finds it (§6.5's padding rule; the signer the platform uses at the device's SDK), equals a build attested by that many independent builders. | T48 attestation clause; crafted-APK suite from real apksigner output; MC-D15, MC-D21, MC-D26, MC-D27 |
| CS-D3 | **No rollback.** Within a trust epoch, the root version and the `(root_version, manifest_seq)` pair never decrease; the floor never decreases within a root version and never exceeds the highest attested release of its manifest; nothing at or below the installed `versionCode` is offered; equivocation at one pair is detected and kept until a newer root, and a second validly chained root at a held version is kept for the epoch's life. A new epoch begins only at the first start of a newly installed APK whose embedded root is not on the stored chain. | T49; MC-D6, MC-D7, MC-D16 … MC-D18, MC-D25, MC-D28; emulator downgrade case |
| CS-D4 | **Bounded freeze and bounded input.** No offer, directory change or mirror change comes from an expired manifest; every fetched object has a size bound fixed before reading; every update run asks at least one mirror the trusted root lists, so neither a mirror nor a stolen release key can hold the client below the newest bundle a root-listed mirror serves. | T50; MC-D8, MC-D13, MC-D19, MC-D24, MC-D30; hostile model mirrors |
| CS-D5 | **Identical from two environments.** The release outputs of a tagged commit have identical SHA-256 in environment A and environment B. | T12 (extended); `release-build.yml` |
| CS-D6 | **Signing keys never online.** No signing secret is in the repository, in CI or on a mirror; signing happens on the offline host, and `core(signed) = unsigned` holds for every published APK. | T52; G-D1; the RK1 checklist |
| CS-D7 | **Key loss and compromise are recoverable** within the declared bounds: any single token, any single root key, the release keys (by a root key's log leaf if both release tokens are gone), the log and witness sets and the attestation keys rotate without a new app identity; a stolen release key captures nothing permanently, because RK4's new root reaches every updater through the root's anchor mirrors and resets the floor and conflict scopes; loss of all three root keys, or a 2-of-3 root compromise, is recovered by a recovery APK that starts a new trust epoch (manual install, L10-D13); the Android key survives total token loss through its shares. | rotation drills (§11.5), the stolen-release-key and recovery-epoch worlds (§11.2), RK5 |
| P-D1 | **Update traffic is independent of activity.** Which job runs are update runs is a function of the process's random key and run index only; whether a job runs at all belongs to the OS and to app visibility, as for quiet runs (a job that fires while the app is visible returns); every update run lasts the same time and asks for the same objects whatever the client's state, so an offer or a pending download changes neither its length nor the timing of later jobs. | T51; NI-U1, NI-U2, NI-U3; PM-D1, PM-D6, PM-D11, PM-D12 |
| P-D2 | **Onion-only, own scope.** Every update fetch goes over Tor to an onion listed by the trusted root or a fresh verified manifest (the embedded root's mirrors on `NO_CHANNEL`), on the `Update` isolation scope, never during a background relay session, a quiet run or an issuer call; a user-requested check or download runs while the app is visible and may overlap a foreground relay session (declared, L10-D20). | T51, T6; PM-D2, PM-D10 |
| P-D3 | **Uniform requests.** Every client in an update run asks for the same objects with the same headers; no request carries a version, root version, identifier or conditional header; the native side builds every path and range from verified state. | T51; NI-U3, NI-U4; PM-D3 … PM-D5, PM-D8, PM-D9 |
| P-D4 | **Rollout without identifiers.** The rollout bucket is per release (it hashes the APK's SHA-256 and the release's fixed salt), derived from a secret that never leaves the device. | T51; PM-D7, PM-D13 |
| P-D5 | **Rollout without a wire partition.** During a staged rollout, relays and the issuer see the same wire behaviour from updated and not-updated clients: a release whose relay- or issuer-visible behaviour changes ships it dormant and activates it for everyone at one ES week (Q46); the client's HTTP/2 SETTINGS and request shapes are pinned by a golden transcript. | NI-U5; G-D11 |

| ID | Deviation | From | Recorded in |
|---|---|---|---|
| X-D1 | Update checks happen in activity-independent update runs and at the user's request, not at app start | FR-7.5 "startup update checks" (spec p.10) | ADR-51 |
| X-D2 | APKs come only from onion mirrors | spec §12.2 step 4 "HTTPS/IPFS" (already ADR-07; restated) | ADR-52 |
| X-D3 | The binary transparency log is the "release-registry commitment" | spec §12.2 step 2 (an on-chain registry, removed by ADR-02) | ADR-49 |
| X-D4 | No authorised downgrade; recovery is roll-forward | spec §12.2 step 3 "unless an emergency recovery policy explicitly authorizes them" | ADR-51 |
| X-D5 | Multi-party access for the root and recovery, single-party (owner) release signing | FR-8.2 "multi-party access" (spec p.11) | ADR-48 |
| X-D6 | "repo F-Droid/Accrescent" decided and documented, not built in Phase 14 | plan:318, ADR-37 point 7 (Accrescent) | ADR-52 |
| X-D7 | Effort ≈ 9–10 calendar weeks for a team of 5–6 (≈ 27 person-weeks), not 3–4, and a gate bounded by owner actions with lead times (§13.2) | plan:318 | §13.2, Appendix B.1 |
| X-D8 | `REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES` in the T15 allowlist; one non-exported installer-status receiver (T15m) | T15 (`INV:23`, `common.sh:15`), `AndroidManifest.xml:5` | ADR-51 |
| X-D9 | A new crate in `libghost_client_net.so` and a new closed JNI class | ADR-19 (consistent with it; recorded) | ADR-51 |
| X-D10 | One file outside SQLCipher: the staged APK under `noBackupFilesDir/update` | the T20 discipline (`INV:28`), applied by analogy | ADR-51 |
| X-D11 | The phase gate's "două medii" = two vendor environments (workstation, hosted runner); the auditor's environment (FR-8.9) is a release gate | plan:318 read with FR-8.9 (plan:278) | ADR-50 |
| X-D12 | Schema migration numbers follow merge order, not reservation | F11 §13.6 rule (c) (`faza11-media.md:893`) | §9.6, Q34 |
| X-D13 | Staging mirrors run by one party until a second operator exists | spec §12.2 step 1 "independent locations", TM:165 | ADR-52 |
| X-D14 | `vcsInfo` off; `versionCode` from a committed release counter; `.stagenet` suffix | `app/build.gradle.kts:11,14-15` | ADR-50, ADR-52 |
| X-D15 | Client native libraries built in a dedicated `client-release` profile (line-table debug info, split, stripped after link, GNU build-id); `[profile.release]` and every `bin/*` stay as they are | `ghost/Cargo.toml:97-99` (`strip = "symbols"`), `build-native.sh` | ADR-50 |
| X-D16 | Per-release signing by one person; the K1 "two people" rule kept for genesis and root changes only (and, on stagenet, the owner alone with a recorded checklist, Q7) | the K1 practice (`faza8-issuer.md:456`) | ADR-48 |
| X-D17 | A release that changes relay- or issuer-visible behaviour is staged with that behaviour dormant; the change activates for everyone at one ES week (or the release goes 0 → 1000 without stages) | FR-8.8 and C-12's staged rollout read as applying to the binary, not to a wire change (P-D5) | ADR-51 |

### 0.4 What this design deliberately does not do

*Phase 13 forward note:* consumed by Phase 13 §6.7; release notes need a format field or are withdrawn (F-U11); the dynamic T6 test includes an update run (F14 §10.2); Q16 (no silent updates) unchanged.

- **Self-update UX** (screens, the "Install unknown apps" prompt, alarms for STALE and CONFLICT, release notes): Phase 13. Phase 14 delivers a UI-less facade (§9.5).
- **Bridges in the manifest**: the field is reserved and must be empty in v1 (§4.5); Phase 12 fills it and must also embed a default set, since a manifest cannot be fetched while Tor is blocked (F-D7).
- **The mainnet genesis** (RK0 for network 1) and network selection at build: with the mainnet K1, Phase 16/17 (`ADR-22:29`). Phase 14 runs the full chain on stagenet.
- **Operating the rollout** (choosing percentages, health signals, the rollback threshold): Phases 17–18 with the runbook of §7.6; Phase 14 builds the mechanism and rehearses it.
- **The audit itself and the auditor's first attestation**: Phase 15 and a release gate.
- **Accrescent submission, an F-Droid-format repository, Google Play, delta updates, APK Signature Scheme v3.2 (ML-DSA hybrid, Android 17+), multi-device, desktop clients, open-sourcing** (ADR-37; Q61 of F9).
- **Any telemetry or health metric collected from clients** (ADR-06).
- **Changing the ES format** (GHES v2, hash-then-sign): F-D1, before the mainnet K1.
- **Root expiry** (TUF §5.3.10): the manifest's expiry and the root-listed mirrors bound the freeze instead (D7); a yearly two-person ceremony only to keep the root alive is not added.
- **Scheduling directory changes per week inside the client** (a `directory_week` field): routine relay changes already travel with the ES's week schedule, so only an emergency withdrawal changes current relay sets, and that at once (§7.8; residue L10-D21).

---

### 1.2 The exit gate, read precisely

The gate has four parts, and this design closes each with stated evidence (§11.7, §13.3):

1. **Tamper drill.** Every tampered root, manifest, proof, bundle and APK is refused, by the Rust core (vectors and single-byte mutation: every mutation of a tight vector is refused, and a mutation of redundant material — an extra signature or cosignature, a root older than the trusted one — leaves the decision unchanged, §11.1), by the JVM update world against hostile model mirrors, on an emulator with the real installer, and once **live** on a real phone over the real staging mirrors and the real log (D26: the gate build with a drill root drives the updater; the RK1-signed stagenet releases are installed by adb for the platform's half).
2. **Downgrade drill.** A rolled-back manifest, an older root, a release at or below the installed `versionCode`, an APK below the floor: refused or not offered; the platform's own refusal of a lower `versionCode` is shown on the emulator and on the phone.
3. **Rotation drill.** On stagenet with the real tokens (RK2, RK3, RK5): a release-key rotation, a root-key rotation (its log leaf signed by a root key), an Android-key rotation through a v3.1 lineage (signed in one run by the original and the new token, §6.5) installed over the previous release on API 33+ and on API 29 (where the old key keeps working, L10-D3), a witness-set change, an attestation-key change, a mirror retirement, and the emergency revocation of a release key reaching a client whose manifest mirrors the stolen key had replaced.
4. **"Hash identic din două medii".** The closing tag's release outputs (§6.1) have identical SHA-256 in environment A (the owner's workstation) and environment B (a GitHub-hosted runner), both built from the committed recipe.

**Not proved by the phase gate (release gates, §13.3 last row):** the auditor's reproduction and attestation (T12b, FR-8.9's second environment), the independent monitor's signed results, the production log policy on mainnet, a second independent mirror operator, the Android developer verification decision, the project identity's legal holder and the new public repository, counsel's items (§16), the licence screen (Phase 13).

### 1.3 Non-goals and deferrals (each with the constraint that decides it)

*Phase 13 forward note:* consumed by Phase 13 §6.7; the update screens follow revision 2 (no in-app "verify this app", D25).

| Item | Why not here | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Update screens, permission prompt, release notes | UX is Phase 13 (plan:317) | Phase 13 on the §9.5 facade |
| Bridges in the manifest | a blocked Tor cannot fetch a manifest; needs an embedded set first (ADR-16) | Phase 12 (F-D7) |
| Mainnet genesis and network selection at build | follows the mainnet K1 (`ADR-22:29`) | Phase 16/17 (RK0 on network 1) |
| Accrescent flavor and APK sets | no admission possible today (§8.2) | S-store, when the conditions of §8.2 hold (Q26) |
| F-Droid-format repository | no verifier covers it (Q71) | not planned |
| Delta updates | each delta is another artifact to reproduce, attest and log | not planned |
| APK Signature Scheme v3.2 (ML-DSA hybrid) | Android 17+ only; no hardware token signs ML-DSA; needs new classical and PQC keys | Q33 |
| Health telemetry | ADR-06 | manual signals only (§7.6) |
| Root expiry | the manifest's expiry and the root-listed mirrors bound the freeze (D7); a root that expires needs a two-person ceremony every year | not planned |
| An update hold around user-requested checks | closing relay sessions for 20–60 minutes after a manual check costs every manual check the user's messages | declared instead (L10-D20; Q47) |

### 1.4 Contradictions between documents and how this design resolves them

1. **FR-8 numbering.** In the spec's §4.8 table the ids, the requirement texts and the acceptance criteria come in the same order in the extracted text (spec p.11): FR-8.1 reproducible, signed, hash and SBOM ("Release pipeline verifies provenance and dependency lock"); FR-8.2 offline/HSM keys ("Key compromise and rotation drill completed"); FR-8.3 telemetry; FR-8.4 declarative infrastructure; FR-8.5 incident response; FR-8.6 security contact; FR-8.7 claims; FR-8.8 staged rollout. The plan (§7, "CP-10 și FR-8.7"), TM S9 ("SBOM (FR-8.1)") and ADR-07 ("Înlocuiește … FR-8.1") agree; **ADR-37 point 8** cites FR-8.2 for the SBOM, **ADR-38's header** completes "FR-8.2, FR-8.8", and **F9 §2.10.6** cites FR-8.8 for UI claims. This design uses the spec's order and proposes the three errata of Appendix B.5 (citation fixes; no decision changes).
2. **FR-7.5 "startup update checks"** against T19 and T23: a check at app start tells the mirror when the app opens. Resolved by update runs (X-D1).
3. **"Pinned release key"** (spec §12.2 step 2): the pinned object is the root (D3), which survives a lost release key; the release key is pinned through it.
4. **"Emergency recovery policy"** (spec §12.2 step 3): Android refuses a lower `versionCode` (developer.android.com, "Version your app"), and an uninstall loses the local data. Resolved by roll-forward (X-D4).
5. **Accrescent** is a channel in ADR-37 point 7 and Q71's default, but its rules (no self-updater, APK sets, app-ID domain verification) and its closed sign-up make it unreachable now (X-D6, §8.2).
6. **"Două medii"** (plan:318) and FR-8.9's auditor: no auditor exists before Phase 15; the phase gate uses two vendor environments, the auditor's is a release gate (X-D11).
7. **LIM:158** ("distribuția prin F-Droid repo/onion mirror") and **TM S10** ("F-Droid repo cu build reproductibil") predate ADR-37: Appendix B.3 and B.4 rewrite them.
8. **"Phase 14 may make the release key sign the ES directly"** (`faza8-issuer.md:463`): not adopted, because GHES v1 signs its whole body, which a token cannot (D6, F-D1); the release record logs the ES instead (D19).
9. **Migration numbers**: F11 reserves 8 after Phase 10's 6 and 7; schema versions must be contiguous on a device, so whichever phase merges first takes the next number (X-D12).
10. **Staged rollout** (FR-8.8, C-12's 5/25/50/100) against **T2 and the relays' allowed observables**: a release whose wire behaviour differs makes its first stage a cohort every relay and the issuer can see. Resolved by the wire rule (X-D17, P-D5).
11. **ADR-39 point 4** (a project identity for public material) against **what the build publishes today**: the APK's notices preamble names the owner (`licenses/apk-notices-preamble.txt:4`, assembled at `app/build.gradle.kts:156`) and `ghost/Cargo.toml:34` names the personal GitHub repository. Resolved by D25 and G-D9 before the first distributed APK.

---

## 2. Threat and privacy model of the distribution chain

### 2.1 Assets (TM AS-10 split; Romanian rows in Appendix B.3)

| ID | Asset | Where | If compromised | If lost |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AS-10a | `K-APK`, the Android signing key, per network | tokens T1, T2 (no other key on them) + SLIP-39 shares (§3.3) | anyone can sign APKs Android accepts as GHOST updates **outside** the updater (sideloads); the updater still needs a logged manifest (and attestations); an APK install can also start a new trust epoch (§7.4 step 0), which adds nothing to what code signed by `K-APK` can already do | without shares: a new app identity, every user reinstalls and loses local data (identity recoverable from the mnemonic; tokens not, `LIM` E11) |
| AS-10b | `K-REL1`, `K-REL2` | tokens T5, T6, separate from the `K-APK` tokens and with their own PINs | can sign manifests: metadata and availability attacks until RK4 — relay withdrawals, extra mirrors (never removing the root's anchor mirrors), rollout changes, a floor up to the highest attested release of its manifest (any listed release on stagenet, where `attest.t` = 0), a `CONFLICT` alarm — all logged; none survives RK4's root, which every updater reaches through the anchor mirrors; no code execution without AS-10a | root update replaces them; a root key signs that root's log leaf (§4.3) |
| AS-10c | `K-ROOT1…3` | token T3, SLIP-39 shares at three custodians, second party's token T4 | with 2 of 3: replaces every other key; the new root is logged under the previous root's policy before any client adopts it (§4.3), so the swap is public | 1 of 3: root update; 3 of 3 lost, or 2 of 3 compromised: recovery only through a recovery APK whose embedded root starts a new trust epoch, installed outside the updater (§3.5) |
| AS-10d | `K-ATT-*` (auditors) | the auditor's machine | false attestations; harmless without AS-10a/b | root update |
| AS-10e | `K-SCHED` (existing, ES) | offline, raw 32-byte file (`protocol/entitlement/README.md:20-28`) | can sign tagging ES versions; they reach clients only inside logged APKs | new key pinned in the next APK |
| AS-10f | mirror onion keys | mirror hosts | impersonation of that mirror (serves only what a mirror can: signed bytes or nothing) | an extra mirror: retired by the next manifest; an anchor mirror: by RK2 |
| AS-10g | `K-TAG`, the git signing key | hardware-backed (FIDO2 SSH key) | signed tags for malicious commits; caught only by review and the auditor's reproduction of the source it read | new key in the allowed-signers file |
| AS-10h | `K-RL`, the Sigsum rate-limit key | owner's workstation, DNS TXT | none for trust (rate limiting only) | new key, new TXT record |
| AS-10i | the release record (archive) | mirrors | none (public, hash-bound by logged manifests) | republish |

### 2.2 Adversaries (TM §3)

AD-1 and **AD-V** are one party for new versions (`TM:49,56`): the vendor can sign, log and publish anything. The design does not prevent a malicious vendor release; it makes it **public and attributable** (log), **reproducibility-checked** (attestations, once an auditor exists) and **logged before anyone's updater accepts it**: every manifest any updater accepts is in the public log and archive. It does not make every user see the same manifest at the same moment — a vendor can log a manifest and serve it only for a short window — so the monitor records what the mirrors actually served and for how long, and an independent party runs it (§5.3). Other parties: a **malicious mirror operator** (AD-2 class), **AD-9** (supply chain: dependency, toolchain, CI), **AD-12** (legal compulsion, which can also reach the owner's release key and mirrors), **AD-4** (local network), **AD-8** (device), **relays and the issuer** (they observe the wire, so a staged release can partition by version, P-D5), **log and witness operators**, the **auditor**, **Google** (the platform's developer verification, whose verifier may look installs and updates up, and Play Protect on GMS devices), **GitHub** (it hosts the private repository and environment B), the **domain registrar and DNS host**, **store operators** (Accrescent, if ever used).

### 2.3 Scenarios (each → mitigation → residue → test)

| ID | Scenario | Mitigation | Residue (LIMITE L10) | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DS-1 | CI or a self-hosted runner is compromised (anyone who can push runs code with sudo, `ci-self-hosted.md:192-196`) | CI holds no key (T52); CI outputs only hash lists (text); the signed APK is the workstation build, signed only if equal to the hosted runner's hashes; signing on the offline host | a compromise identical in A and B (a poisoned pinned input) passes (L10-D7) | G-D1; `release-build.yml` |
| DS-2 | The owner's workstation is compromised (source changed before tagging; A's files or B's sums substituted on the way to the signer) | signed tags with a hardware-backed key; B's `SHA256SUMS` digest confirmed on an independent device at RK1 step 2, so A's files must equal what the hosted runner built; the auditor reproduces from the source it reviews (release gate) | before an auditor exists, a malicious source change made before tagging is not detected by reproducibility (L10-D7; L-SRC); an owner who skips the independent-device comparison loses the A = B check against this attacker | tag verification in G-D4; RK1 step 2 |
| DS-3 | The offline signer host is compromised | read-only verified boot image; it only checks hashes and asks tokens to sign (PIN, touch); `core(signed) = unsigned` checked again online before logging; monitors compare logged APKs with attested builds | it could get a wrong-but-valid-looking manifest signed if the owner approves without reading the RK1 output (L10-D11) | RK1 checklist; monitor |
| DS-4 | A malicious mirror serves stale, garbage, endless or split data | signatures and log proofs; size bounds; two mirrors per run, at least one listed by the trusted root, highest `(root_version, manifest_seq)` pair; equivocation flag; expiry; an expired manifest changes no directory or mirror | a freeze up to the expiry if every reachable mirror is stale (L10-D4) | T50; hostile model mirrors (EXPIRED_REPLAY) |
| DS-5 | A release key is compromised (theft, or AD-12 compulsion of the owner) | it cannot make an installable APK; every manifest it signs must be logged and is seen by monitors; its manifests cannot remove the root's anchor mirrors, cannot set the floor above the highest attested release they list, cannot relabel relay operators; RK4's new root reaches every updater through the anchor mirrors, makes the stored manifest non-current and resets the floor and conflict scopes | until RK4: relay withdrawals, extra mirrors, rollout changes, a floor at the highest attested release (any listed release on stagenet), a `CONFLICT` alarm — all logged (L10-D11) | the stolen-release-key world (§11.2); MC-D3, MC-D24, MC-D25, MC-D31, MC-D32 |
| DS-6 | `K-APK` is compromised | the updater still requires a logged manifest and, with threshold ≥ 1, attestations; `K-APK` shares no token or PIN with a release key (one stolen token never gives both); RK3 rotates with a v3.1 lineage; the floor forces the rotated release | sideloaded malicious APKs are accepted by Android outside the updater; on API 29–32 the old key keeps working after a rotation targeted at 33, and every later release is also signed by it (L10-D3, L10-D12) | emulator rotation cases |
| DS-7 | Keys are lost, or two root keys are compromised | SLIP-39 shares for `K-APK`; root threshold 2 of 3; a root key can log the next root (no release token needed); a recovery APK starts a new trust epoch (§7.4 step 0), which neither an older nor a higher-numbered attacker root can override | all three root keys lost, or 2 of 3 compromised: updaters follow the old chain until the user installs the recovery APK outside the updater (verified with `ghost-verify`) (L10-D13) | RK5 drill; the recovery-epoch world |
| DS-8 | Downgrade (manifest, root, APK) | §7.5; platform `versionCode` | a fresh install of an old, validly signed APK is accepted by Android; the app reports UNSUPPORTED after its first update run (L10-D5) | T49; emulator |
| DS-9 | Targeted build (AD-V, AD-12) | every accepted manifest logged; equivocation detection across mirrors and by monitors; an independent monitor that records what each mirror served and for how long (a logged manifest served only briefly is flagged); attestations | detection after the fact, not prevention; first installs and store channels outside the updater (L10-D1, L10-D2) | T48; monitor rule (f) |
| DS-10 | The log or witnesses misbehave or disappear | client verification is offline; split views need the log and a witness quorum to collude; proofs do not expire | the default policy's witnesses share a parent company (L10-D6); no new manifest can be logged while every policy log is down; a root change needs a log of the previous policy (L10-D17) | MC-D11, MC-D12 |
| DS-11 | The auditor is compromised or coerced | attestations bind only the reproduced bytes; useless without AS-10a and AS-10b | collusion of auditor and vendor is not covered | — |
| DS-12 | Supply-chain compromise (AD-9) | Gradle checksums (and signatures, Q22), cargo-deny, `--locked`, crate allowlists, pinned toolchains, SBOM | an upstream compromise of a pinned version before disclosure (TM S9) | T7; G-D2 |
| DS-13 | Update-channel metadata | update runs (T51) of fixed duration, not held by the payment hold; onion only; uniform requests; paths and ranges built natively; no logs at mirrors (G-D10) | the mirror sees update-run fetches and APK downloads, i.e. "a client updates now" and the APK size (the version); a download resumed over several runs links those runs by its ranges; app visibility at a job's time decides whether that job runs, as for quiet runs (L10-D8); mirror operators estimate the install base and the adoption curve from fetch rates, and a retired mirror still running sees only clients with an old list (L10-D22) | T51; NI-U1 … NI-U4 |
| DS-14 | The local network sees the download | Tor | a Tor download of about `apk_length` bytes soon after a public release marks a likely GHOST user (LIM L4 amended, L10-D14) | — |
| DS-15 | Google on GMS devices (Play Protect, developer verification) | none possible in the app; disclosed; de-Googled systems recommended in Phase 13 copy | the platform may send app details or the APK to Google at install; the developer verifier checks installs (reportedly updates too) against package name and signing-key hash, from a cache or by a lookup to Google; from 2027 unregistered apps need the "advanced flow" on certified devices (L10-D9, L10-D10, L10-D24) | V-D21 (whether a self-updater can pass a pre-auth token) |
| DS-16 | Store channels (if Accrescent is ever used) | none (no updater in a store build) | store updates bypass log and attestation; the store learns installs (L10-D2) | — |
| DS-17 | Operators run unverified relay binaries | `ghost-verify operator`; operator images built from the released binary | an operator who does not check (L10-D15) | S8 |
| DS-18 | Device clock manipulated to revive an expired manifest, or honestly wrong | decisions about time only under the sync engine's clock trust (READY ⇒ within σ = 3 days of true time, F7:58, the bound `relay_client.rs:277` attributes to Arti — whose own defaults accept a consensus up to 1 day early and 3 days late, `tor-dircommon` 0.46.0 `config.rs:139-157`, while a cached consensus can stretch the slow side; ±1 h step rule, F7:1655); "not yet valid" bounds at σ + 1 day; heartbeats 14 days before expiry | up to 3 days around expiry (L10-D4) | MC-D8; slow- and fast-clock cases of T50 |
| DS-19 | Denial of service on the mirrors (an introduction flood) | PoW defenses kept for browsers; two root-listed mirrors per run; mirrors of two operators in production | GHOST clients do not solve PoW (`infra/relay/torrc`, `rust-feature-policy.sh`), so under a flood updaters wait in the zero-effort queue until the flood ends or the manifest expires, while Tor Browser users get through (L10-D18; Q40) | — (declared) |
| DS-20 | A staged release partitions clients by version for relays and the issuer | the wire rule (P-D5): wire changes dormant until one ES week; the client's HTTP/2 SETTINGS pinned; a golden wire transcript decides each release's wire flag | timing differences that are not wire behaviour (for example a changed retry rule) are not covered by the transcript (L10-D23) | NI-U5; G-D11 |
| DS-21 | Public material names the owner (notices, SBOM `repository` fields, CI links, tag messages, the domain's registrant, a Google developer account) | D25: a project identity (provisional from S3; the legal holder with counsel, Q45); no CI links or tags in the release record; G-D9 scans every output | what was public before the repository turned private stays public (ADR-39 point 1); registration with Google, if chosen (Q18), ties a legal identity to the package (L10-D10) | G-D9 |
| DS-22 | The publication leaks the owner's network location (a Go tool under `torsocks`; a mirror on the home connection) | R-P runs in a container whose only route is a Tor client (`HTTPS_PROXY` for `sigsum-submit`, never `torsocks`); a packet capture in V-D11b; the hosting rule of §7.1 | GitHub sees environment B's builds before publication; the registrar, the DNS host and the log operators see the domain linked to the leaves and the submission times (L10-D25) | V-D11b; G-D10 |

### 2.4 Who sees what in the update channel (Romanian rows in Appendix B.3)

| Event | Mirror (onion) | Local network | Relays, issuer | Log, witnesses | Google (GMS device) | Device (AD-8) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Update run | at an activity-independent time, one circuit fetches `current.ghrb` from each of two mirrors (at least one root-listed); same path, headers and bytes for every client with the same stored state; a fixed duration | Tor traffic for the run's fixed length | — (no relay session, no issuer call in an update run; the run's length does not depend on an offer, so later relay sessions do not either) | — | — | the latest manifest, proof, root chain and the facts of §9.6 |
| APK download (update runs) | a circuit fetches `apk/<sha256>` in 4 MiB ranges: "a client updates to the offered version"; resumed ranges link the runs of one download (L10-D8) | Tor traffic totalling about `apk_length` soon after a public release: a likely GHOST user (L10-D14) | — | — | — | the staged file until installed |
| Install or update | — | — | after a **wire-neutral** release, nothing new; a wire change is dormant until its ES week (P-D5) | — | Play Protect may send app details or the APK (L10-D9); the developer verifier checks the package and signing-key hash from its cache or by a lookup to Google, reportedly on updates too (L10-D24) | the platform's install records |
| User "check now" or download (Phase 13) | as an update run, at the user's moment (declared) | Tor | may overlap a foreground relay session: an operator of both a relay and a mirror can line them up (L10-D20) | — | — | — |
| First install (Tor Browser) | an HTTP/1.1 fetch of the index and `apk/<sha256>` on the browser listener: a population distinct from the app by protocol and headers | Tor Browser traffic | — | — | as Install | — |
| Release publication (owner) | receives files over its upload onion (a separate Tor instance) | Tor traffic from the publication container only | — | the submission over Tor, the leaf's time; the leaf is public | — | — |

**Observers outside the client's traffic.**

| Observer | Learns | Residue |
|---|---|---|
| Mirror operators, in aggregate | fetch rates: the install base and, after each release, the adoption curve (no individual) | L10-D22 |
| A retired mirror that keeps running | only clients whose stored state still lists it: an update-latency partition | L10-D22 |
| GitHub | every environment-B release build, its timing and hashes, before publication (private repository; ADR-39) | L10-D25 |
| The domain's registrar and DNS host | the registrant (project identity, D25) and the `_sigsum_v1` record that links the domain to the submissions | L10-D25 |
| Log operators and witnesses | each leaf's time and the rate-limit domain; nothing about clients (clients never contact the log) | L10-D25 |
| The public repository's host | who fetches `ghost-verify` and the fingerprint (Tor reachable, Q28) | — |
| Google (developer verification, Play Protect) | see the table above; the limited-distribution route would register each tester's device with Google (§8.5) | L10-D9, L10-D24 |
| Store operators (if ever used) | installs and updates (L10-D2) | L10-D2 |

### 2.5 The property the gate proves

For a client running a GHOST build from the log, under the assumptions A3 (Tor) and the hardness of Ed25519, SHA-256 and the APK signature: (i) it installs, through its updater, only APK bytes listed in a manifest signed by the release threshold of its trusted root (the newest root reachable from its trust anchor by the chain rule) and included in a policy log under a quorum-cosigned checkpoint; (ii) when the root's attestation threshold is k ≥ 1, only APKs whose unsigned core k independent builders attested; (iii) within a trust epoch its root version and its `(root_version, manifest_seq)` pair never go back, its floor never goes back within a root version, and it never offers a version at or below its own; (iv) which of its jobs are update runs is a function of its process key and run index, and what an update run fetches is a function of the public manifest and root it holds. **Against AD-V** the property is detection: a targeted or malicious release reaches updater users only after appearing in a public log (and, with k ≥ 1, after an auditor attested a build of it); every manifest any updater accepts is in the public log and archive, and one served only briefly is flagged by the monitor (§5.3 rule (f)). **Against a stolen release key** the property is recovery: RK4's root reaches the client through a root-listed mirror at its next update run. **It says nothing** about first installs not checked with `ghost-verify`, store updates, operators who do not check their binaries, the platform's own reporting to Google, timing differences between versions that the wire transcript does not capture (L10-D23), or the correctness of reviewed source (L-SRC).

### 2.6 Claims (CP-10; ADR-38 point 11)

Allowed in Phase 13 copy only after the gate closes and with the evidence named: "Actualizările sunt semnate, înregistrate într-un jurnal public cosemnat de martorii numiți în politica <nume> și, de la <versiune>, reproduse de <auditor>." Always beside **both** residues: ADR-38 point 11's mandatory sentence, "Nu puteți verifica singuri codul sursă; vă bazați pe auditorii numiți și pe jurnalul public.", and "Prima instalare și actualizările din magazine nu trec prin această verificare; folosiți verificatorul public." Forbidden: "sigur", "imposibil de falsificat", "verificat de oricine", "open source", "sursă deschisă", "auditat" without a named report, "martori independenți" while the policy's witnesses share a parent company (L10-D6), "reproductibil" without a named attestation, any statement that Google does not learn of installs or updates. **Required**, as a factual disclosure and not a guarantee: the Play Protect and developer-verification paragraph of §8.5 (F-D8). The app shows no "verify this app" value of its own (an in-app check proves nothing, D21); the facade exposes none (§9.5).

---

## 3. Keys, roles, custody and ceremonies (normative; ADR-48)

### 3.1 Roles and algorithms

| Key | Algorithm | Signs | Lives | Threshold | Rotation | Backup |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `K-APK` (per network) | ECDSA P-256, self-signed X.509 certificate (subject `CN=GHOST`, no personal name; the certificate's DER bytes are part of the identity and are committed, `protocol/release/<network>/apk-signer.crt`) | the APK Signing Block (v2 + v3; after a rotation, v2 and v3.0 by the original key and v3.1 by the new one, §6.5) | tokens T1, T2 (PIV slot 9c, PIN always, touch always; no other key on them) | — | RK3 (v3.1 lineage), on compromise only | SLIP-39 3-of-5 of the 32-byte scalar |
| `K-REL1`, `K-REL2` | Ed25519 | `GHRM` statements; the Sigsum leaves of manifests (and of roots, as an alternative to a root key) | generated on T5 and T6 respectively (PIV slot 9c), non-exportable; their own PINs, never the `K-APK` tokens (Q48) | 1 of 2 | RK2 | none needed (root replaces; a root key logs that root) |
| `K-ROOT1` | Ed25519 | `GHRT` statements; the Sigsum leaf of the next root | token T3 (owner) | 2 of 3 | RK2 | none (threshold) |
| `K-ROOT2` | Ed25519 | `GHRT` statements; the Sigsum leaf of the next root | SLIP-39 2-of-3 paper shares of its 32-byte seed at three custodians, the owner holding at most one (stagenet: Q43); signed with on the offline host only | 2 of 3 | RK2 | the shares are the key |
| `K-ROOT3` | Ed25519 | `GHRT` statements; the Sigsum leaf of the next root | token T4, held by a second party (Q7) | 2 of 3 | RK2 | none (threshold) |
| `K-ATT-<name>` | Ed25519 | `GHRA` statements | each independent builder's own machine | root's `attest` threshold | RK2 | the builder's |
| `K-SCHED` (existing) | Ed25519 | the ES (`"ghost/v1/entitlement-schedule" ‖ body`) | offline, raw seed (stagenet key outside the repository today) | — | pinned per network in `ghost-entitlement` | unchanged by this phase (F-D1) |
| `K-TAG` | Ed25519 (OpenSSH `sk-ssh-ed25519`, FIDO2 on a token) | release tags | owner's token | — | allowed-signers file | second token |
| `K-RL` | Ed25519 | Sigsum rate-limit tokens | owner's online workstation | — | DNS TXT `_sigsum_v1.<domain>` | none needed |

**Why two algorithm families.** APK Signature Scheme v2 accepts RSA (1024 … 16384 bits), ECDSA (P-256, P-384, P-521) and DSA, not Ed25519 (source.android.com, "APK Signature Scheme v2"); the manifest keys follow the ES precedent (Ed25519, `ed25519-dalek` 2.2.0 already in the client, `ghost/Cargo.toml:75`) and Sigsum's leaf signatures (Ed25519). **Why P-256 and not RSA-4096:** both are in the v2 list and in YubiKey PIV; P-256 signs faster on a token and gives the same security level for this purpose; RSA-3072 is not in the v2 size list. RSA-4096 stays the recorded alternative (Q6).

### 3.2 Hardware and the offline signer host

- **Tokens.** YubiKey 5 series with firmware ≥ 5.7 (PIV Ed25519 and X25519, key import, move and delete; docs.yubico.com, "YubiKey 5.7 firmware specifics"). Firmware cannot be upgraded, so the tokens are bought with 5.7+. **Six per network**, one role per token: T1, T2 (`K-APK`), T5, T6 (`K-REL1`, `K-REL2`), T3 (`K-ROOT1`), T4 (the second party's `K-ROOT3`). One stolen token and its PIN therefore yield either the Android identity or a release key, never both (with `attest.t` = 0 on stagenet, both together would be a fully valid, logged malicious update); the release tokens are stored apart from the `K-APK` tokens. Coupling them on four tokens is the cheaper alternative, declared if chosen (Q48). PIV PIN and management key changed from defaults at RK0, a different PIN per token; touch policy "always" for every signing slot.
- **Signer host.** A dedicated laptop whose radios are disabled in firmware, never networked, booted from a **read-only, signature-verified Debian live image** (the image's `SHA256SUMS` and its signature checked on the online workstation, written to write-protected media). Tools on a second write-protected medium, each checked against its SHA-256: `ghost-release-ops` (§9.1) against `SHA256SUMS` from the two build environments (§6.4); build-tools 37.0.0 (`apksigner`, `zipalign`), a JDK 17 runtime (apksigner is Java; JDK 21 needs extra flags for SunPKCS11, fdroidserver issue 1313), Yubico's `ykcs11` or OpenSC (PKCS#11), `yubico-piv-tool` and `python-shamir-mnemonic` against the digests recorded in `infra/builder/pins.txt`. Inputs and outputs travel on a separate medium that is wiped after each ceremony.
- **PKCS#11 paths (checked in S0b, V-D6, V-D7).** `apksigner sign --provider-class sun.security.pkcs11.SunPKCS11 --provider-arg <cfg> --ks NONE --ks-type PKCS11` is the documented token path (it is example 5 of build-tools 37.0.0's own `apksigner sign --help`, read locally on 2026-10-03). Ed25519 statements are signed through PKCS#11 (`CKM_EDDSA`) or `yubico-piv-tool`; ykcs11 gained the Ed25519 key type in yubico-piv-tool 2.5.0 (release notes), and Yubico's ykcs11 page lists `C_SignInit` with `CKM_EDDSA` (firmware 5.7+ for Ed25519; checked 2026-10-03); V-D7 confirms it on the tokens bought. **Fallback** if V-D7 fails: the release and root Ed25519 seeds live encrypted on the signer host's media (never on a networked machine), with the same thresholds; recorded in ADR-48.
- **Why short statements matter here.** A third-party project reports that YubiKey PIV refuses Ed25519 messages above about 1 KiB (search result, unverified; V-D7 measures). Every GHOST statement is ≤ 60 bytes and every Sigsum leaf statement 56 bytes (§4.2), so the limit never applies; the ES, which signs its whole body (`schedule.rs:282-284`), stays a software key (F-D1).

### 3.3 Backups and the multi-party rule

- **`K-APK`** is generated on the signer host (OpenSSL, P-256), its certificate created there once (validity per V-D19), the key and certificate imported into T1 and T2, the raw scalar split by **SLIP-39** (status Final; reference implementation `python-shamir-mnemonic`, MIT, run on the signer host) into **3-of-5** shares: owner's home safe, a bank deposit box, two trusted persons, counsel or an escrow agent. The scalar never exists elsewhere; the signer host's RAM is the only place it is assembled (RK0, RK5).
- **Root.** Threshold 2 of 3: `K-ROOT1` (the owner's T3), `K-ROOT2` (SLIP-39 2-of-3 shares at three custodians; the owner holds at most one, so using `K-ROOT2` needs at least one custodian to release a share at a recorded ceremony) and `K-ROOT3` (the second party's T4). No single person can rewrite the trust root **as long as** the custodians release shares only at such a ceremony; the owner acts with `K-ROOT1` and either the second party or a custodian. The second party and the custodians can act without the owner (`K-ROOT2` + `K-ROOT3`), because a root key signs the next root's log leaf (§4.3): the owner's tokens are not a single point of failure. Submitting that leaf still needs a Sigsum rate-limit token for the domain, so RK0 gives the second party a copy of `K-RL` (a rate-limit key only, no trust, AS-10h). On mainnet the format itself requires `root.t ≥ 2` and at least three root keys (§4.3). **Stagenet** may use the simplified custody of Q43 (the owner holds every share in separate places; declared: on stagenet the owner alone can rewrite the root).
- **Per-release signing is single-party** (the owner with T1 or T2 for the APK and T5 or T6 for the manifest). This is X-D5: FR-8.2's "multi-party access" is met for the trust root and for recovery, not for routine releases, because a solo owner must be able to ship a fix alone; the log makes every release public, which is the check a second signer would give.

### 3.4 Ceremonies (runbook `ghost/infra/release/RUNBOOK.md`, Romanian, each step with its expected output)

| Ceremony | When | Who | What | Output |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **RK0 genesis** | once per network (stagenet in this phase; mainnet with the mainnet K1, Phase 16/17) | mainnet: owner + witness (second party); stagenet: the owner alone with the recorded checklist (a witness if Q7 names one) | generate `K-APK` and its certificate, import to T1/T2, SLIP-39 shares; generate `K-REL1` on T5, `K-REL2` on T6, `K-ROOT1` on T3, `K-ROOT2` seed (shares), `K-ROOT3` on T4; write the anchor mirrors; sign root v1 with all three root keys | `root-1.ghrt`, `apk-signer.crt`, public keys; signed checklist |
| **RK1 release** | each release | owner | verify inputs (two `SHA256SUMS`, B's digest confirmed on an independent device, unsigned APK, attestations, manifest source, the archive index); `apksigner sign` (PKCS#11; both `K-APK` keys after an RK3); `ghost-release-ops apk-core` (`core(signed) = unsigned`); build the manifest, sign it with a release token, sign the Sigsum leaf statement and write the add-leaf request | signed APK, `GHRM`, add-leaf request |
| **RK2 root update** | key rotation, witness or log change, attestation keys, APK signer list, anchor mirrors | two of three root key holders | build root v(n+1); sign with ≥ 2 old and ≥ 2 new root keys; log its leaf under the **old** root's policy, the leaf signed by a root key **or** a release key of the old root, and assemble its `GHRP`; then an RK1-style manifest under the new root | `root-(n+1).ghrt` and its proof, a new manifest |
| **RK3 Android-key rotation** | on compromise of `K-APK` only (no periodic rotation: after a rotation targeted at API 33, every later release needs the original key too while `minSdk` < 33, §6.5; Q9) | owner + witness | new `K-APK'` on two new tokens as in RK0; `apksigner rotate` (the old signer's `rollback` capability off — apksig's default); RK2 adds the new certificate digest and keeps the old one while `minSdk` ≤ 32 (§7.4 step 9) | lineage file, `root-(n+1).ghrt` |
| **RK4 compromise response** | on suspicion | owner (+ root holders) | a release key: revoke by RK2 (drop the key; the new root reaches every updater through the anchor mirrors, voids the stored manifest and resets the floor and conflict scopes); `K-APK`: RK3; raise the floor; two root keys: the recovery APK of §3.5; publish a notice in the archive; Phase 13 shows CONFLICT or the floor | new root and manifest (or a recovery APK) |
| **RK5 restore drill** | yearly (`TM:89`) and at RK0 | owner + one share holder (stagenet: the owner, Q43) | restore `K-APK` from three shares onto a blank token on the signer host, compare public key and certificate, sign a test APK, wipe; restore `K-ROOT2` and sign a test statement | signed drill record |
| **RK6 heartbeat** | at least every 77 days (14 days before `not_after_day`; with each quarterly K1 when it falls earlier) | owner | re-sign the current manifest with sequence + 1, new dates and the same release entries (salts unchanged); log it | `GHRM`, add-leaf request |

**RK1 in steps** (the runbook's form: each step names its expected output; any other output stops the ceremony, as runbook K1 does, `infra/issuer/RUNBOOK.md` §5.1):

1. On the online workstation: `git verify-tag v<code>` against `protocol/release/<network>/allowed_signers` → "Good signature"; `release-build.yml` for the tag green; the local environment-A build done. Copy to the transfer medium: the unsigned APK, `SHA256SUMS-A` (local), `SHA256SUMS-A` (job) and `SHA256SUMS-B` (job, from the run's summary), the manifest source, the current root chain, the attestations received, and `archive-index.txt` (every logged manifest's pair, hash and releases, §5.3) fetched from two mirrors over Tor and compared.
2. On the signer host: `ghost-release-ops sums-compare A-local A-job B-job` → `SUMS_EQUAL files=<n> b_digest=<first 16 hex digits of SHA-256(SHA256SUMS-B)>`; the owner compares `b_digest` with the same value read **on an independent device** (the owner's phone, from job B's summary, where job B prints it; never through the workstation): a workstation that forged B's sums is caught here, and any difference stops the ceremony; `sha256sum -c` of the APK against the sums → `OK`.
3. `apksigner sign …` (§6.5) with T1 or T2 (PIN, touch; after an RK3, the original and the new `K-APK` in one run) → no output; `apksigner verify -v --print-certs --min-sdk-version 29 signed.apk` → "Verifies", "Verified using v3 scheme (APK Signature Scheme v3): true" and the certificate digest of the root (after an RK3 also "v3.1 … true" and both digests). apksigner reports the v2 scheme as "false" at `--min-sdk-version` ≥ 28 although the v2 block is present (measured, build-tools 37.0.0, Appendix C.3), so v2 is not an expected line; `apk-core` checks the block IDs itself.
4. `ghost-release-ops apk-core --apk signed.apk --expect <unsigned sha256>` → `CORE_EQUAL padding=<p> blocks=<ids>` (§6.5's rule).
5. `ghost-release-ops manifest-build --source … --root-chain … --apk signed.apk --attestations … --archive-index archive-index.txt` → `MANIFEST_BUILT seq=<s> root=<r> releases=<n> sha256=<…>`; it refuses (`MANIFEST_REFUSED <reason>`) a counter not above every logged one, an `issued_day` below the previous manifest's, an ES ending before the release week + 25, a missing ES slot onion, an ES-slot relay of the current or next six weeks withdrawn without the run naming it an emergency withdrawal, a changed operator id for a known onion, an ES week whose slots span fewer than two operators, a floor above the highest attested release listed, releases with different `min_sdk`, a salt changed for a logged `apk_sha256`, a wire-flag inconsistent with the wire transcript or a wire-changing release at a permille strictly between 0 and 1000, an attestation that does not verify, a missing escrow digest (kind 16).
6. `ghost-release-ops statement --manifest …` → the 59-byte statement file; sign it on a release token (T5 or T6); `ghost-release-ops attach` → `SIGNED keys=<k>/<t>`.
7. `ghost-release-ops leaf-statement` → the 56-byte Sigsum statement; sign it on the same token; `ghost-release-ops leaf-request` → `LEAF_REQUEST message=<sha256>`.
8. Copy back: signed APK, manifest, add-leaf request. Wipe the transfer medium's copy on the host; power off. Continue with §5.2.

### 3.5 Rotation and compromise, per key

- **Release key** (lost or compromised): RK2 removes it (both release tokens lost: a root key signs the new root's log leaf, §4.3); clients adopt the new root from the next bundle of a root-listed mirror — which every update run asks, so a stolen key's manifests that listed only attacker mirrors cannot keep it out — refuse manifests signed only by the removed key whatever their sequence, stop treating the stored manifest as current (it names the old root), and start new floor and conflict scopes. Malicious manifests signed before that are in the log.
- **Root key**: one lost or compromised key is replaced by RK2 with the other two. **All three lost, or two compromised:** the updater cannot follow a chain the vendor controls, and against two stolen root keys no version number can win (the attacker can publish v(n+1), then roots naming its own log, faster than any embedded recovery root). Recovery is a **recovery APK** (signed by `K-APK`, so Android accepts it as an update) that embeds a new root chain — given the next unused version number for readability, not because order decides anything — installed outside the updater and verified with `ghost-verify` against the new root published in the public repository and on the mirrors. At its first start the client finds that its stored root is not on the embedded chain and starts a **new trust epoch** from the embedded root (§7.4 step 0); bundles on the old chain then never verify. This adds no attack surface: whoever holds `K-APK` already runs arbitrary code in the app. Clients that never install the recovery APK stay on the old chain (L10-D13); the runbook RK4 includes the public notice and the recovery APK's publication.
- **Android key** (compromised): RK3 with `--rotation-min-sdk-version` default 33. Devices on API 33+ that install the rotated release refuse later updates signed by the old key (capability `rollback` off: apksig `SigningCertificateLineage`, default flags exclude `PAST_CERT_ROLLBACK`). Devices on API 29–32 keep verifying the original signer in the v3.0 block (source.android.com, "APK Signature Scheme v3.1"; measured, Appendix C.3: the v3.0 block carries the original key for SDK 24–32, the v3.1 block the new key for SDK 33+), so a sideloaded old-key APK still updates them (L10-D3), **and every later release must be signed by the original key as well** (apksigner refuses otherwise while `minSdk` < 33, measured): two `K-APK` keys at every RK1 for as long as `minSdk` stays below 33. `--rotation-min-sdk-version 28` with v2 off rotates API 28–32 too and signs with the new key alone (measured), but key rotation is "not recommended for Android 12 (API level 31) and earlier" (source.android.com, v3) — Q9. Raising `minSdk` to 33 removes both the residue and the dual signing at a device-coverage cost (Q9).
- **Android key** (lost, shares intact): RK5's path, then continue. Shares also lost: a new identity (L10-D13); the transparency log and the public repository announce the new certificate.
- **Log, witnesses, attestation keys**: RK2.
- **Mirror onion key**: an extra mirror is retired by the next manifest; an anchor mirror by RK2 (clients drop it when they adopt the root).

### 3.6 Networks

`network` is a signed byte everywhere (1 mainnet, 2 stagenet, 3 regtest for vectors only), as in the ES (`schedule.rs:1-30`). Each network has its own root, its own `K-APK` and certificate, its own mirrors and its own `applicationId` (stagenet: suffix `.stagenet`, applied to the release build type of the stagenet configuration only, so the `debug`, `gate` and test packages keep the names `scripts/device/dm-gate.sh:80` and `dm-emulator-run.sh:87` hardcode; Q3's rename changes those scripts, Appendix B.6). A client uses the root of its embedded ES's network and refuses anything of another network (`WRONG_NETWORK`). Phase 14 runs RK0 for stagenet only. The live drill's **drill root** (D26) carries the stagenet byte but is a separate chain with software keys, embedded only in `gate` builds and served at its own path (§7.2); G-D4 refuses it in any release build.

---
