Every real stagenet RK1 or RK6 of this phase — S7's and S9's releases, the live drill's real releases, the rollout rehearsal, the roll-forward and any rotation that produces a manifest — falls after that date, so the owner signs **stagenet ES seq 2** first — at a K1 ceremony, as the issuer runbook already requires before the first release after week 2964 (`infra/issuer/RUNBOOK.md:254-256`), with the stagenet schedule key and the material that lives outside the repository (`%USERPROFILE%\.ghost-stagenet-keys\`, `RUNBOOK.md:71`), then published by K2 to the issuer and every staging relay (`RUNBOOK.md` §5.2): OA-11, Q38, before S7's real release and S9. S2's rehearsal runs on regtest (network 3), which needs no real ES.

---

## 8. Distribution channels and application identity (normative; ADR-52)

### 8.1 The onion mirror (primary)

Built in this phase (§7.1). First installs: Tor Browser opening `http://<onion>/` on a mirror's HTTP/1.1 listener and downloading `apk/<sha256>` from the index page (V-D22 checks that Tor Browser for Android downloads and installs it from a staging mirror), or any channel at all (a friend's phone, a USB stick, a messenger) **followed by `ghost-verify`** on a computer; integrity does not depend on the channel, only on the verifier (D21). No clearnet mirror (D1).

### 8.2 Accrescent: facts and the entry conditions

Facts (accrescent.app docs and FAQ, 2026-10-03): "Apps do not need to be open-source to be submitted"; "Accrescent doesn't build, sign, or update developers' apps itself"; signature schemes v2, v3 or v3.1; **multiple signing certificates not supported**; **"Accrescent does not accept monolithic APKs"** — uploads are APK sets built with bundletool from an app bundle; ≤ 1 GiB; target SDK per Google Play's rules, with removal on non-compliance; **"Apps may not have self-updaters"**, including "improper use of the REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES permission … downloaders which prompt the user to update from an APK"; sign-up "only permitted for allowlisted GitHub accounts"; **domain verification** of the app ID's domain by a DNS TXT record `_accverify.<domain>` (for `com.example.securechat.android`, `example.com`). The research digest's "bundletool ≥ 1.11.4" is not on the requirements page and is not repeated here. Accrescent's client has no documented Tor setting; what its servers learn per update check is undocumented (L10-D2).

**Entry conditions** (all must hold before S-store starts; Q26): (a) the owner's GitHub account is allowlisted (OA-8); (b) the `applicationId` lies under a domain the project controls (Q3, Q4); (c) a **store flavor** without the `:update` module and without `REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES` (the updater lives in its own Gradle module so the flavor is a dependency switch, §9.1); (d) a reproducible `bundleRelease` and a deterministic `bundletool build-apks` whose splits are signed by `K-APK` through PKCS#11 (bundletool's own signing takes a keystore; the splits may need `apksigner` one by one — S-store measures), with the APK set's hashes in `SHA256SUMS` and attested; (e) the residue declared and shown (store updates bypass the log and attestations; the store learns installs).

### 8.3 An F-Droid-format repository

Not built (Q71's applied default). Facts: the main F-Droid repository accepts only FLOSS ("All applications in the repository must be Free, Libre and Open Source Software", Inclusion Policy) and points other apps to "a separate repository"; a repository is defined by its own index-signing key (Security Model), signs its index (index-v2 with SHA256withRSA), recommends that "the repo signing keys should not ever be on a public server" (Setup an F-Droid App Repo); F-Droid 2.0 (2026-09-24) moved "Use Tor" to generic proxy settings, recommends Tor VPN and installs updates by default through the session installer. An own repository would add a third key family (RSA index key) and its updates would bypass GHOST's log check like any store; users would need Orbot or a Tor VPN for an onion repository. Reconsider only if a verifier can cover it (Q71).

### 8.4 Google Play (rejected)

Google Play holds the app signing key (new apps are enrolled in Play App Signing with Google-generated keys; a developer-provided key is also held by Google; support.google.com 9842756): against A7, TB-6 and FR-8.2, and a Play-signed GHOST could not update a mirror-signed one or the reverse. Publishing also discloses the developer's legal identity for personal accounts (channels digest; not re-checked here, Appendix C). Not a channel.

### 8.5 Android developer verification and Play Protect (platform facts)

- **Developer verification** (developer.android.com/developer-verification and its FAQ, 2026-10-03): from **2026-09-30**, installs from participating stores in Brazil, Indonesia, Singapore and Thailand on certified devices (Android 7+) require registered developers; in **2027** it "expands globally for all apps on certified Android devices"; for now, "if users sideload your app directly, these new verification requirements won't apply to your app yet"; "Unregistered apps can only be installed or updated when the advanced flow is enabled or by using ADB so if the advanced flow is disabled updates to unregistered apps will fail"; registration takes package names and signing-certificate fingerprints, a legal name, a government ID and a fee; a limited-distribution account (no ID, no fee) covers at most 20 devices; "If you lose your signing key you won't be able to register your packages". The advanced flow (Developer options, screen-lock authentication, a no-coercion confirmation, a restart and a 24-hour wait, then 7 days or indefinitely; press reports of 2026-08) is a large burden for a privacy app's users.
- **The verifier looks installs up.** The platform's Android Developer Verifier checks an install against the package name and the signing key's hash, from a local cache of popular apps or, otherwise, by a lookup to Google; app stores can pass a "pre-auth token", a "cryptographically verifiable blob" tied to the package, to avoid the lookup (Android Authority, 2025-10-02). A third-party analysis reports that "the verification check is made on all installs, even updates" (agnostic-apollo, updated 2026-09-28); Google's FAQ says only that updates of unregistered apps fail without the advanced flow. Under enforcement, a GHOST self-update may therefore tell Google that this device updated GHOST, whatever the Play Protect settings (L10-D24). Whether a self-updater can pass a pre-auth token (reportedly through `PackageInstaller.SessionParams#setExtensionParams`) is assumed, not verified: V-D21 checks it in S0a.
- **The limited-distribution account is not "no identity".** It needs a Google Account with 2-Step Verification and a Google payments profile holding a legal name and address; it authorizes up to 20 devices, each by a code generated on the device after its user confirms they personally know and trust the named developer (support.google.com/android-developer-console 17131204). Using it for testers would register their phones with Google; it is not suggested for Phase 16's 20–50 testers either way.
- **Consequence:** from 2027, on certified devices, the onion mirror works for unregistered GHOST only through the advanced flow or ADB; registration ties the owner's legal identity to the package and certificate at Google. GrapheneOS and other non-certified systems are outside Google's stated scope [I]. Decision for the owner with counsel **before Phase 16 onboards its alpha testers** (plan row 16: 20–50 testers on stagenet; Google's global step "in 2027" may already apply by then) (Q18); this phase only keeps `K-APK` backed up so registration stays possible.
- **Play Protect** on GMS devices: "If you install apps from unknown sources …, Google Play Protect may ask you to send unknown apps to Google"; with "Improve harmful app detection" on, it sends them automatically; a scan "will send app details to Google for a code-level evaluation" (support.google.com 2812853). A GHOST install or update may therefore tell Google that a device installed GHOST (L10-D9). Phase 13 copy says so and suggests the setting and de-Googled systems.

### 8.6 Application identity

`applicationId` is permanent once a release ships: Android binds data, updates and signatures to it. `org.ghost.app` (`app/build.gradle.kts:11`) names `ghost.org`, which the project does not hold; Accrescent's domain check and developer verification's "package name already in use … additional review" make that costly later. Q3 asks the owner to fix the final id (with the domain of Q4) **before the first distributed APK**; stagenet appends `.stagenet` (D2).

---

## 9. Integration: modules, JNI, `:sync`, schema (normative)

### 9.1 Module layout

| Where | What | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `ghost/client-core/update` (`ghost-client-update`) | formats (§4), the trust-anchor rule and the decision (§7.4), the APK parse and core hash, the mirror draw, the rollout bucket, `GHRA` | pure: no I/O, no clock, no logging, no async runtime, `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`; depends only on `sha2`, `ed25519-dalek` 2.2.0 and `hmac`, all already in the client graph; **one new entry in `client-core/rust-dependency-allowlist.txt`**, the crate itself (the list names workspace crates too, `rust-dependency-allowlist.txt:119-123,415`); linked into `libghost_client_net.so`; the G-R8-style scan (G-D6); a `mutants` feature (the `test-vectors`/`pq-spy` pattern of `client-core/dm/Cargo.toml`) that only `ghost-dm-host` enables and G-R2's check keeps out of every shipped graph (§11.3) |
| `ghost/client-core/net` | `mirror.rs` (`MirrorClient::over_tor`, HTTP/2 GET on `IsolationScope::Update`, bounds, deadlines, paths and ranges built from a mirror-set handle); the embedded root chain (`include_bytes!`); pinned HTTP/2 SETTINGS for the mirror, relay and issuer clients (§7.2); the JNI exports of §9.3 | onion only through `connect_isolated` (the clippy bans of `client-core/net/clippy.toml` unchanged) |
| `ghost/release/ops` (`ghost-release-ops`) | the offline and publication tool: `root-build`, `root-verify`, `manifest-build` (the release-time checks of §3.4 step 5, §7.5, §7.6, §7.8, §7.9), `statement` (prints the bytes to sign), `attach` (adds a signature), `leaf-statement`, `leaf-request` (a Sigsum add-leaf request body from a token signature), `apk-core` (§6.5's rule), `proof-assemble`, `bundle` (with `archive-index.txt` and the index page), `sbom`, `sums-compare` (with `b_digest`), `export-public`, `verify` | workspace member; prints only through a fixed vocabulary (the `ghost-issuer-ops` `report.rs` pattern); **holds no key** — signatures come from the token tools (§3.2); no network code (submission is `sigsum-submit`) |
| `ghost/public/` → the new public repository | `ghost-verify` (§5.4) on a copy of `ghost-client-update`, the black-box kit's manifest and native-library checks, exported by `export-public` at each release (artifact kind 19) | permissive licence (Q28); reproducible from its source; the project identity only (D25, G-D9) |
| `ghost/android/network` | `UpdateNative` (verification calls) and `TorMirrorTransport` (the fetch), thin wrappers decoding fixed layouts strictly | the `EntitlementCrypto` pattern (`EntitlementCrypto.kt:3-11`) |
| `ghost/android/update` (`:update`, new) | `UpdateEngine` (the update-run participant), `UpdateStore` (migration U's DAO), `ApkStager`, `InstallerPort` (`PackageInstaller`), the facade `Updates` for Phase 13 | no protocol logic (ADR-19); no `java.net` (the `kotlin-clearnet.sh` scope extended) |
| `ghost/android/sync` | update runs (§9.4) | T19, T23 unchanged in substance |
| `ghost/android/storage` | migration U (§9.6) | the F9 §13.6 practice |
| `ghost/android/app` | wiring; the bootstrap relay insert (S5a); `REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES`; the installer-status receiver; `vcsInfo` off; the release counter; the `.stagenet` suffix on the stagenet release build type only; a `-keep` rule for the `Updates` facade (the gate instrumentation drives it in the R8-shrunk build before Phase 13 calls it) | T15, T15m |
| `ghost/android/app/src/gate` | the drill instrumentation (D26): `requestCheck`, `requestDownload`, `install`, UI Automator on the platform's confirmation, `Updates.status` and result codes written to the instrumentation output | `gate` build type only (G-D4: no drill root in a release build) |
| `ghost/infra/{builder,mirror,release}`, `ghost/scripts/release/` | the recipe; mirror deployment; `RUNBOOK.md` (Romanian: RK0 … RK6, R-P, R-R); `build-release.sh`, `sums-compare.sh`; the publication container (§5.2) | G-D2, G-D10 |
| `ghost-dm-host` | gains the `UpdateNative` exports and the `mutants` switch for the JVM worlds | G-R6's lists gain `UpdateNative`, `TorMirrorTransport` and the test-only mutant symbol (as F10 and F11 add theirs) |
| `ghost/scripts/device/` | `dm-gate.sh:80` and `dm-emulator-run.sh:87` hardcode `org.ghost.app.gate` and `org.ghost.app.test`; D2's suffix leaves them, Q3's rename changes them; `update-drill.sh` (new) | Appendix B.6 |

### 9.2 `ghost-client-update` API (normative signatures)

```rust
/// One vocabulary for §7.4, the JNI results, `ghost-verify` and the tests ("each must give its exact Code").
pub enum Code { Ok, Malformed, WrongNetwork, ClockUntrusted,
                RootChainBroken, RootUnlogged, RootConflict, RootMismatch, BadSignature, FloorInvalid,
                LogProofInvalid, QuorumNotMet, NotYetValid, Rollback, Unchanged, Conflict,
                NoManifest, Stale, SdkUnsupported, Unsupported, UpToDate,
                ApkLayout, ApkLength, ApkHash, ApkCore, ApkSigner, AttestationQuorum, OutOfOrder }
pub enum Day { Trusted(u32), Untrusted }                       // an untrusted clock is a value, not a missing check
pub struct Stored<'a> { epoch: [u8; 32], epoch_version_code: u32,
                        chain: &'a [u8],                       // GHRC from the epoch's first root to the trusted root
                        manifest: Option<&'a [u8]>, manifest_root: u32, manifest_seq: u64,
                        manifest_sha256: Option<[u8; 32]>,
                        floor: u32, floor_root: u32, conflict_root: Option<u32>, root_conflict: bool }
pub fn start(stored: &Stored, embedded_chain: &[u8], installed: u32) -> Start;   // §7.4 step 0
pub fn accept(stored: &Stored, bundle: &[u8], today: Day, now_unix: Option<u64>) -> Accepted;
                                                               // steps 1–6; step 7 is Kotlin's transaction
pub fn offer(stored: &Stored, installed: u32, sdk: u8, rollout_key: &[u8; 32], today: Day) -> Offer;  // step 8
pub fn mirrors(stored: &Stored, embedded_chain: &[u8], no_channel: bool, today: Day,
               draw: &[u8; 32]) -> Result<MirrorPair, Code>;  // §7.8: first from the anchors, second from anchors ∪ extras
pub struct ApkCheck { /* streaming state */ }
pub enum Need { Footer { offset: u64 } }                       // the 24 bytes before cd_offset
impl ApkCheck {
    pub fn begin(stored: &Stored, release: u8, sdk: u8, length: u64,
                 tail: &[u8; 22]) -> Result<(Self, Need), Code>;           // step 9 (a): the native side reads the EOCD
    pub fn footer(&mut self, bytes: &[u8; 24]) -> Result<(), Code>;       // step 9 (b), (c)
    pub fn update(&mut self, offset: u64, chunk: &[u8]) -> Result<(), Code>;  // in order only; (d), (e)
    pub fn finish(self) -> Result<(), Code>;                               // step 10
}
pub fn directory(stored: &Stored, today: Day) -> Result<Directory, Code>; // fresh, current manifests only (§7.8)
pub fn ghra(network: u8, release: &Release) -> Vec<u8>;
```

Kotlin passes the file's length and last 22 bytes, then reads exactly the range `Need` names, then streams; it never locates or parses ZIP structures itself (RQ-D12).

### 9.3 The closed JNI surface (gate G-D5) and result codes

`org.ghost.network.UpdateNative` exports exactly: `nativeEmbeddedChain`, `nativeStart`, `nativeAccept`, `nativeOffer`, `nativeDirectory`, `nativeMirrorSet` (builds, from the stored state, the embedded chain, the day, the run's draw and the offered release if any, a **mirror-set handle** holding the two chosen canonical onions, the network's path prefix and the offered `apk_sha256` and `apk_length`), `nativeMirrorSetClose`, `nativeApkBegin`, `nativeApkFooter`, `nativeApkUpdate`, `nativeApkFinish`, `nativeApkAbort`; `org.ghost.network.TorMirrorTransport` exports `nativeFetch(transport, mirrorSet, slot, kind, chunkIndex, deadlineMs)`, where `slot` ∈ {0, 1} picks one of the set's two mirrors, `kind` ∈ {BUNDLE, APK_CHUNK}, and the native side builds the path and the 4 MiB range itself, refusing a chunk index beyond `apk_length` — Kotlin can name neither an onion nor a path nor an offset (revision 1's `nativeFetch(handle, onion, path, rangeStart, …)` took all three from Kotlin; mutants PM-D9, PM-D10). `EntitlementCrypto` gains `nativeEmbeddedRelays` (S5a, §7.8). Byte in, byte out; fixed big-endian result layouts decoded strictly in Kotlin (a wrong length is `malformed_response`); results carry a one-byte `Code` (§9.2) and never a message; APK checks live in the ADR-19 handle registry (opaque ids, idempotent abort, `catch_unwind`). The fetch fails with the existing `NetworkException` categories (`transport`, `deadline`, `malformed_response`, `closed`, …; no new category unless S4 shows one is needed, then `ErrorPolicyTest` and `client-core/README.md` change together). G-D5 is **G-R6 extended**, not a second mechanism: `dm-jni-surface.sh`'s `OTHER_JNI_CLASSES` (`dm-jni-surface.sh:76`, today `TorRelayTransport TorIssuerTransport EntitlementCrypto`, which refuses any other `Java_org_ghost_network_*` symbol) gains `UpdateNative` and `TorMirrorTransport` with their closed lists, its fixtures gain a positive and a negative case for each, the test-only mutant switch joins the symbols allowed only in libraries passed with `--test`, and a new rule checks that only `android/update` and `android/network` reference `UpdateNative`; the method stays `llvm-nm -D --defined-only` on the shipped library (`ci.yml:234-240`).

### 9.4 `:sync` additions (normative)

1. `RandomSources.updateRun(index)`: a new keyed domain of the process's random key; `JobFate` gains `UPDATE_RUN`, and `JobFate.of(occupied, quietDraw, updateDraw, participant, updater)` decides in the precedence of §7.3: `RETURN` if occupied, else `QUIET_RUN` if the quiet draw holds and a participant is installed, else `UPDATE_RUN` if the update draw is `< 1/24` and an updater is installed, else `BACKGROUND_SESSION` (`JobFate.kt:34-38`). `SyncRuntime.runJob` starts an update run before its `relayHeld()` check: the payment hold holds relay sessions only.
2. `SyncController.setUpdater(UpdateParticipant?)`, one slot, set at wiring (the participant pattern, `SyncController.kt:20-25`).
3. `UpdateParticipant.onUpdateRun(session: UpdateSession)`; `UpdateSession` grants `mirror: MirrorAccess` only, with `clockTrusted()`, the run's fixed end (`D_U` after READY) and `closed` (the `LeasedSession` contract: never inside a sync transaction, never feeding breakers or budgets); the runtime keeps the job until the fixed end even when the participant returns early.
4. `SyncController.runUserUpdateCall { … }`: only while the app is visible, never during a background session or a quiet run (the `runUserIssuerCall` rule, which may run it beside a foreground relay session: declared, L10-D20, Q47).
5. No change to the read lane, to quiet runs or to relay sessions: an update run, like a quiet run, simply has no relay session; T19's tests gain the update draw as a pure function, and NI-U1 and NI-U3 show the read schedule and every later job's timing unchanged by update state.
6. **Other phases' worlds that model the job decision** change with the fourth outcome: Phase 9's `PrivacyWorld` calls `JobFate.of` for every job (`PrivacyWorld.kt:447`; F9 §22.36 item 12 requires worlds to use the production decision), the Phase 8 T2 world models "a normal run is a background relay session" (`issuer/crates/service/tests/t2/world.rs:5-12`), and the entitlement harness (`NiK`) and `JobFateTest` follow the same model. Passing "no updater" would break the world = production rule; passing "updater installed" turns 1/24 of the non-quiet runs into runs without a relay session, shifting the S-DM2 and S-DM4 baselines, the JD7 windows and T2's NI-1/NI-1d views. Q39's default: the worlds take the production decision with an updater installed; slice S6b re-runs their gates at gate scale (the T2 gate ≈ 2 h 49 min; each DM privacy pair ≈ 49 min) and records the new baselines under Phase 9's +0.02 rule.

### 9.5 `:update`: engine, states, facade

States per offered release, and channel flags beside them (`STALE`, `CONFLICT`, `ROOT_CONFLICT`, `UNSUPPORTED`, `SDK_UNSUPPORTED`, `NO_MANIFEST`, and `NO_CHANNEL` when no mirror answered in 12 consecutive update runs — a counter, `upd_state.failed_runs`, so no time column is needed). Every legal and illegal transition has a JVM test (the F9 §13.5 practice); the persisted part is `upd_staged.state` (§9.6), the rest is derived at start from the stored facts.

| From | Event | To | Side effects |
|---|---|---|---|
| `NONE` | an update run or a user check accepts a manifest whose offer (§7.4 step 8) is non-empty | `OFFERED` | none (no fetch triggered by acceptance itself) |
| `OFFERED` | an update run (or the user) starts the download | `DOWNLOADING` | `upd_staged` row `partial`, file `<sha256>.part` |
| `DOWNLOADING` | a 4 MiB range written | `DOWNLOADING` | `received` advances in the same transaction as the file sync |
| `DOWNLOADING` | last range written and §7.4 steps 9–10 return `OK` | `STAGED` | rename to `<sha256>.apk`; row `verified` |
| `DOWNLOADING`, `STAGED` | the check fails, or a newer accepted manifest no longer offers this release | `NONE` or `OFFERED` (the new offer) | file and row deleted |
| `STAGED` | `install()` (Phase 13, user present) | `INSTALLING` | session created; row `committed` only after `apkFinish = OK` and `session.commit` |
| `INSTALLING` | status receiver: success, then the new process sees its own `versionCode` = the release's | `INSTALLED` → `NONE` | file and row deleted |
| `INSTALLING` | status receiver: failure or user abort | `STAGED` (file kept) or `FAILED(code)` → `OFFERED` | session abandoned |
| any | wipe (Phase 3) | — | tables and `noBackupFilesDir/update` deleted |

```kotlin
interface Updates {                                   // UI-less, for Phase 13
    val status: UpdateStatus      // UpToDate | Available(name, code) | Downloading(received, total) | Ready(name, code)
                                  // | Installing | Installed(code) | Stale(day) | Unsupported(floor) | SdkUnsupported
                                  // | NoManifest | Conflict | RootConflict | NoChannel | Failed(code)
    val manifestIssuedDay: Int?   // issued_day of the newest manifest held: public data, not a time of activity (§9.6)
    fun requestCheck(): CheckOutcome            // user-initiated, foreground only (§7.3)
    fun requestDownload(): DownloadOutcome      // user-initiated, foreground only
    fun install(): InstallHandle                // §7.7; the platform confirms
}
```

Revision 1's `signerCertificateSha256()` "for the 'verify this app' screen" is removed: it was the in-app self-check D21 rejects, since a malicious APK would report whatever it liked; verification is `ghost-verify` on another device (§5.4). `manifestIssuedDay` lets Phase 13 say how old the app's release information is without storing when the device last checked.

### 9.6 Schema migration U (normative; one transaction, append-only, fail-closed)

`U` is the next free version at merge: 9 if Phases 10 and 11 merge first with their reserved 6, 7 and 8; whichever lands first takes the next number and the later ones move, because a device applies only versions above its own (X-D12, Q34). The F9 §13.6 rules apply: explicit columns, `typeof` CHECKs, REPLACE guards, write-once and monotonic triggers, no time column of activity (T33's rule; none is needed: `NO_CHANNEL` counts update runs, which are activity-independent, and the only day exposed is the stored manifest's own `issued_day`).

```sql
CREATE TABLE upd_state (
  id                 INTEGER PRIMARY KEY CHECK (id = 1),
  network            INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(network) = 'integer' AND network IN (1, 2)),
  epoch              BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(epoch) = 32),            -- SHA-256 of the epoch's first root
  epoch_version_code INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(epoch_version_code) = 'integer' AND epoch_version_code > 0),
  root_version       INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(root_version) = 'integer' AND root_version >= 1),
  root_chain         BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(root_chain) BETWEEN 64 AND 1048576),  -- GHRC, epoch start .. trusted root
  manifest_seq       INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(manifest_seq) = 'integer' AND manifest_seq >= 0),
  manifest_root      INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(manifest_root) = 'integer' AND manifest_root >= 0),
  manifest_sha256    BLOB    CHECK (manifest_sha256 IS NULL OR length(manifest_sha256) = 32),
  manifest_file      BLOB    CHECK (manifest_file IS NULL OR length(manifest_file) <= 65536),
  proof_file         BLOB    CHECK (proof_file IS NULL OR length(proof_file) <= 16384),
  floor_version_code INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(floor_version_code) = 'integer'
                                             AND floor_version_code BETWEEN 0 AND 2147483647),
  floor_root         INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(floor_root) = 'integer' AND floor_root >= 0),
  conflict_root      INTEGER CHECK (conflict_root IS NULL OR (typeof(conflict_root) = 'integer' AND conflict_root >= 1)),
  root_conflict      INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (root_conflict IN (0, 1)),
  failed_runs        INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(failed_runs) = 'integer' AND failed_runs >= 0),
  rollout_key        BLOB    NOT NULL CHECK (length(rollout_key) = 32),
  CHECK ((manifest_seq = 0) = (manifest_file IS NULL)),
  CHECK ((manifest_file IS NULL) = (manifest_sha256 IS NULL) AND (manifest_file IS NULL) = (proof_file IS NULL))
);
-- triggers: network and rollout_key write-once; no DELETE.
--   An epoch changes only together with epoch_version_code (§7.4 step 0), and then may reset root_version,
--   root_chain, the pair, the manifest, floor, floor_root, conflict_root and root_conflict.
--   Within one epoch: root_version never decreases; (manifest_root, manifest_seq) never decreases
--   lexicographically, and an equal pair keeps its manifest_sha256; floor_root never decreases, and
--   floor_version_code never decreases while floor_root is unchanged; conflict_root is set or raised, and
--   cleared only in an update that raises root_version; root_conflict never returns to 0.
CREATE TABLE upd_staged (
  apk_sha256   BLOB    PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (length(apk_sha256) = 32),
  version_code INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(version_code) = 'integer' AND version_code > 0),
  apk_length   INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(apk_length) = 'integer' AND apk_length > 0),
  received     INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(received) = 'integer' AND received BETWEEN 0 AND apk_length),
  state        TEXT    NOT NULL CHECK (state IN ('partial', 'verified', 'committed'))
) WITHOUT ROWID;                    -- a trigger keeps at most one row
```

#### 9.6.1 Transactions and crash rules (normative)

| Transaction | Steps | Crash rule |
|---|---|---|
| `START` | at the first start of a newly installed version, §7.4 step 0: the one-lineage move or the new epoch, and `epoch_version_code` | all or nothing; repeated at the next start if a crash interrupts it |
| `ACCEPT` | one SQLCipher transaction: `upd_state` (chain, pair, hash, manifest, proof, floor and its root, conflict, root conflict) and, for a fresh current manifest only, the `relay_directory` changes of §7.8 | all or nothing; a crash leaves the previous facts, and the next update run fetches again |
| `RUN-END` | at the end of every update run: `failed_runs := 0` if any mirror answered, else `failed_runs + 1` | a crash loses one count at most |
| `STAGE-CHUNK` | write the range to `<sha256>.part` at its offset, `fsync`, then one transaction advancing `received` | a crash after the write and before the commit re-downloads that range (the file is overwritten at the same offset); `received` never exceeds what is on disk |
| `STAGE-VERIFY` | the full check of §7.4 steps 9–10 on the `.part` file, then `rename` to `.apk`, `fsync` of the directory, then the row to `verified` | at start, a `.apk` with a `partial` row is re-checked; a `.part` with a `verified` row is impossible (the row follows the rename); files without a row are deleted |
| `INSTALL-COMMIT` | stream into the session with the native check, `apkFinish = OK`, `session.commit`, then the row to `committed` | a crash before `commit` abandons the session (the platform's own session cleanup) and leaves `verified`; a crash after `commit` and before the row update is resolved at the next start by comparing the installed `versionCode` |
| `INSTALL-CONFIRM` | at the first start of the new version: the own `versionCode` equals the row's → delete row and file | idempotent |
| `SWEEP` | at start: delete staged files and rows no longer offered by the stored manifest, and any file without a row | idempotent |

The directory change of §7.8 runs on the Phase 7 `relay_directory` table in the same transaction (its `source` CHECK already admits `'manifest'`, `Schema.kt:287`). The migration creates the tables empty; the engine inserts the one `upd_state` row at its first start (the embedded chain as the first epoch, sequence 0, floor 0, the installed `versionCode`), with `rollout_key` drawn from `SecureRandom` (not SQLite's `randomblob`); the key never leaves the database. A wipe (Phase 3) deletes both tables with the rest.

### 9.7 Manifest and permissions (T15, T15m; ADR-51)

- `<uses-permission android:name="android.permission.REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES" />`, with an ADR-51 comment (`AndroidManifest.xml:5` requires one); `T15_ALLOWED_PERMISSIONS` gains it (`common.sh:15`) with the same reference.
- `<receiver android:name="org.ghost.update.android.InstallStatusReceiver" android:exported="false" />`, no intent filter, reached by an explicit, mutable `PendingIntent` (the platform adds the session status extras); `merged-manifest-lint.sh` lists it as G-DM7 lists the alarm receiver.
- No `UPDATE_PACKAGES_WITHOUT_USER_ACTION` (Q16). No `QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES`: the engine reads only its own package's `versionCode`.

### 9.8 Relation to other phases

**Relays and the issuer** get no protocol change: their binaries and the ES file each loads with `--schedule` (artifact kinds 5, 6, 17) enter the release record, and operators check them with `ghost-verify operator` before starting a new version (§5.4); the relay sees nothing of update runs (no relay session in them, and their fixed length leaves later sessions' timing unchanged); the client's HTTP/2 SETTINGS towards relays and the issuer are pinned and golden-tested (§7.2, F-D9), and wire changes follow the rule of §7.6. **Entitlement** is untouched in behaviour: quiet runs keep their 1/8 draw and their single issuer call; the ES stays embedded; the release tool enforces the 26-week rule at release time (§7.9). Phase 13 builds the screens on §9.5 and writes the copy of §2.6 and L10. Phase 12 fills bridges and must embed a default set (F-D7). Phase 10's channel relays come from the same directory. Phase 16/17 runs RK0 for mainnet with the mainnet K1 and adds network selection at build. Phase 15 audits this code and the pipeline (plan:319 "supply chain"). The Phase 9 host library and G-R6 gain the update lists. The Phase 8 and Phase 9 worlds that model the job decision adopt the fourth outcome and are re-baselined (§9.4 item 6, Q39). Phases 9 and 10 get the relay bootstrap early (S5a, F-D11). Phase 16's alpha onboarding waits on Q18.

## 10. Invariants (new T48–T52; changes to T2, T6, T12, T15, T19, T20, T23)

### 10.1 New invariants (Romanian rows for INVARIANTS.md in Appendix B.2)

| ID | Invariant | Verification | Executable from | Code |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **T48** Signed, logged, attested updates | The updater writes into an installer session only bytes equal to an APK listed in a manifest that (a) is signed by the release threshold of the trusted root — the newest root reachable by the chain rule from the trust anchor (the installed APK's embedded root unless the stored root is on its chain, §7.4 step 0), each new root signed by both thresholds and logged under the previous root's policy by a root or release key of that root — (b) has the network and root binding of that root, (c) is included in a policy log under a tree head signed by that log and cosigned by the witness quorum, and (d) whose release's unsigned core, found by the Android-equivalent parse with apksigner's padding rule (§6.5), equals the attested unsigned build with the root's attestation threshold met, and whose signer, selected as the platform selects it at the device's SDK, is listed by the root; size bounds fixed before reading | vectors and single-byte mutation of each format (tight vectors all refused; redundant material decision-neutral); the crafted-APK suite from real apksigner output; the TUF attack list as tests; UpdateWorld with hostile mirrors and the recording installer; mutants MC-D1 … MC-D5, MC-D9 … MC-D15, MC-D20 … MC-D23, MC-D26, MC-D27, MC-D29 | Phase 14 | `client-core/update`, `client-core/net`, `android/update` |
| **T49** No rollback, equivocation kept | Within a trust epoch, the root version and the `(root_version, manifest_seq)` pair never decrease; the floor never decreases within its root version and never exceeds the highest attested release of its manifest; an equal pair with other bytes sets the conflict flag for that root version and changes nothing; a validly chained second root at a held version sets the root-conflict flag; nothing at or below the installed `versionCode` is offered; below the floor, the app is `UNSUPPORTED`; a new epoch begins only at the first start of a newly installed APK whose embedded root is not on the stored chain | trigger tests; JVM sequence suites (rollback, equal-pair conflict, fast-forward and its root escape, floor fast-forward and its root reset); the recovery-epoch world; emulator downgrade; MC-D6, MC-D7, MC-D16 … MC-D18, MC-D25, MC-D28 | Phase 14 | `client-core/update`, `android/storage`, `android/update` |
| **T50** Bounded freshness | No offer, directory change or mirror change comes from a manifest past `not_after_day` (on a trusted clock) or under a root other than the trusted one; every update run reads two mirrors, at least one listed by the trusted root, and keeps the highest pair; neither a mirror nor a stolen release key can hold the client below what a root-listed mirror serves; no time decision on an untrusted clock; time bounds at σ + 1 day | freeze, stale-mirror, EXPIRED_REPLAY and stolen-release-key worlds; slow- and fast-clock cases; MC-D8, MC-D13, MC-D19, MC-D24, MC-D30, MC-D32 | Phase 14 | `client-core/update`, `android/update`, `android/sync` |
| **T51** Private update traffic | Mirror fetches happen only in update runs (which jobs are update runs is drawn from the process key and the run index only; every update run lasts the same fixed time; the payment hold does not hold them) or at the user's explicit request while the app is visible (it may overlap a foreground relay session, declared); on the `Update` scope, never in a background relay session or a quiet run; every update run requests the same objects with the same headers from two mirrors; no request carries a version, a root version, an identifier or a conditional header; the native side builds every path and range (fixed 4 MiB steps) from verified state; the rollout bucket is per release and never leaves the device; a wire-neutral release shows relays and the issuer the same transcript as its predecessor | NI-U1 … NI-U5; request-builder test; scope test in `client-core/net`; the JNI path-and-range test; PM-D1 … PM-D13 | Phase 14 | `android/sync`, `android/update`, `client-core/net` |
| **T52** Keys offline, cores preserved, public material scrubbed | No signing secret is in the repository, in a workflow, in CI or on a mirror; no workflow signs or publishes (apart from G-D1's fixture-key carve-out in `update-emulator.yml`); every published APK satisfies `core(signed) = unsigned` and its unsigned build's hashes were equal in two environments before signing, B's sums confirmed on an independent device; no published file carries a personal identifier of the owner | G-D1 (secret patterns, signing commands in workflows); `ghost-release-ops apk-core` on every release; `release-build.yml` records; RK1 checklist; G-D9 | Phase 14 | `scripts/gates`, `release/ops`, `infra/release` |

### 10.2 Changes to existing invariants

| ID | Change |
|---|---|
| T2 | Unchanged in substance: update runs read nothing of the entitlement state and make no issuer or relay call, so no join key appears through them; NI-U2 shows mirror views identical across entitlement states and the payment hold. **Two consequences recorded:** the fourth job outcome replaces 1/24 of the non-quiet runs' relay sessions, so the T2 world adopts the production decision and its gate is re-run and re-baselined (§9.4 item 6, Q39); and a staged release that changed wire behaviour would partition clients by version at the issuer, which the wire rule prevents (P-D5, NI-U5). |
| T6 | Mirror fetches are onion-only through `connect_isolated`; no clearnet mirror exists. The dynamic T6 test (Phase 13) includes an update run. |
| T12 | "Două medii" becomes executable: the release outputs of a tag are identical in environment A and environment B (`release-build.yml`); the same-path T12 job stays. T12b gains its mechanism: `GHRA` signatures enforced by the updater on networks whose root sets `attest.t ≥ 1`. |
| T15 | The allowlist gains `REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES` (ADR-51); T15m allows `InstallStatusReceiver` (not exported, no filter). |
| T19 | The update draw is a pure function of the process key and the run index; update state never changes the read schedule or a job's length (NI-U1, NI-U3); a job that fires while the app is visible returns, for update runs as for quiet runs. |
| T20 | Unchanged for the sync layer; the update module's one file outside SQLCipher is the staged APK (X-D10). |
| T23 | Update runs are never quiet runs and never contain an issuer call. |

## 11. Testing and the exit-gate argument

### 11.1 Rust: vectors, properties, fuzzing, cross-checks (`rust` job)

- **Vectors** `release_root.txt`, `release_manifest.txt`, `release_proof.txt`, `release_bundle.txt`, `release_apk_core.txt`, `release_onion.txt`: valid cases per format and network; every length and count bound ±1; non-canonical orderings, duplicates, trailing bytes, `ext ≠ 0`, wrong network, wrong statement type; non-canonical onions (a trailing space, uppercase, `+80`, `080`) refused. **Single-byte mutation, with the right oracle:** the acceptance rules deliberately ignore some material — signature-block entries naming unknown keys or failing (§4.2), cosignatures beyond the quorum or by unknown witnesses, chain entries older than the trusted root — so a byte flipped there leaves the decision unchanged, and revision 1's "every single-byte mutation refused" was false (a manifest signed by both release keys under threshold 1 still verifies with one signature flipped). The oracle is therefore: every mutation of a **tight** vector (exactly-threshold signatures, exactly-quorum cosignatures, a chain starting at the trusted root) is refused; every mutation of any other valid vector is refused **or** lies in redundant material and leaves the decision and the stored state byte-identical.
- **Chain rule**: old threshold only, new threshold only, both, a key moved between keysets, a skipped version, a chain with a broken middle link (the last good root kept); a correctly signed new root without its log proof, with a proof under its own (new) policy instead of the previous one, and with a leaf signed by a key of the new root only (`ROOT_UNLOGGED` each); **a root leaf signed by an old root key (accepted)** and by a key of neither old keyset (`ROOT_UNLOGGED`); a chain starting above version 1, and one starting above the trusted root + 1 (`ROOT_CHAIN_BROKEN`); a validly chained different root at a held version (`ROOT_CONFLICT`) and an invalid one (ignored).
- **Trust anchor and epochs** (§7.4 step 0): an APK embedding an older root of the same chain (stored state kept), a newer root of the same chain (moved, nothing reset), a different lineage at a lower version, at a higher version and at the same version with other bytes (a new epoch each); a bundle never starts an epoch.
- **Floor and freshness**: a floor above every attested release, above 2³¹ − 1, and at the highest attested release (`FLOOR_INVALID`, `FLOOR_INVALID`, accepted); a floor raised under one root and reset by the next; an expired manifest with a higher pair (pair and floor stored, no directory or mirror change); a stored manifest under an older root after a newer root (`NO_MANIFEST`); `issued_day` and cosignature times at +4 days (accepted) and +5 (refused); a manifest whose releases differ in `min_sdk` (`MALFORMED`), and a device below the common one (`SDK_UNSUPPORTED`).
- **TUF attack list as tests**: arbitrary installation (APK not in the manifest), endless data (each bound), extraneous dependencies (not applicable: one artifact; stated), fast-forward (a pair pushed to the maximum, then the root escape), indefinite freeze (expiry on a trusted clock), malicious mirrors preventing updates (two mirrors, highest pair), mix-and-match (a proof of another manifest, a root of another network, a manifest of another root), rollback, key compromise below threshold (one of two root keys), wrong software (another network, signer or flavor).
- **Log proofs**: vectors from sigsum-go v0.14.1 (a CI-only Go tool in the `vendor-tests` pattern, BSD-2-Clause, with a Go toolchain pinned by hash): real leaves, inclusion proofs at several tree sizes, signed tree heads, `cosignature/v1` lines; GHOST's parser must agree on every byte (V-D11a).
- **Crafted-APK suite** (committed under `protocol/test-vectors/apk/`, generated once with build-tools 37.0.0 `apksigner` and throwaway keys, which are committed beside them as G-D1 fixtures): **real apksigner output with padding p = 0 and p > 0**; a non-zero byte in the padding; a padding of the wrong length (the block not at `E`'s 4 096 boundary); an EOCD comment; two EOCD records; ZIP64; a missing block magic; mismatched block sizes; a central-directory offset pointing into the block; `cd_offset + cd_size ≠ length − 22`; an extra local entry; a v2-only, a v3, and a v3.1-with-lineage APK signed by the original and the new key, checked **at SDK 29 (the original certificate selected) and at SDK 33 (the new one)**; a v3.1 block whose range does not cover the SDK; a certificate not in the root; a core differing in one byte. Each must give its exact `Code`.
- **Properties**: 10⁶ seeded inputs per parser per run (never panics, never reads past input); the bucket function's distribution (χ² over 10⁵ APK hashes and salts).
- **Fuzzing**: `update-fuzz` targets for every parser and the APK layout, on a dated nightly (the `dm-fuzz` arrangement, F9 §16.2).
- **Independent implementation**: a JVM test-only decoder (JDK `MessageDigest` and BouncyCastle's Ed25519, the F9 §15.2 practice) replays the manifest, root and statement vectors **except the Ed25519 edge vectors**: BouncyCastle 1.85.2 verifies the cofactored equation and disagrees with `verify_strict` on 33 of 53 edge vectors (mixed-order and small-order keys and nonces, `client-core/dm/src/sign.rs:7-9`), so those stay Rust-only; the Merkle path check replays sigsum-go's vectors.

### 11.2 The update world (JVM; `android` job and the gate job)

`UpdateWorld` (`android/update/src/test`): 2–50 clients, each the real `:update` engine over the real `:storage` (SQLite JDBC, migrated to U) and the `:sync` driver with update runs, calling the real native core through the host library; a **job model with the OS's limits** (the Phase 7 app-session and job model, extended with App Standby execution quotas — Active 20 min per 60 on Android 16+, Working set 10 min per 4 h, Frequent per 12 h, Rare per 24 h, Restricted once a day — and the payment hold); **model mirrors** with hostile modes STALE, ROLLBACK, EQUIVOCATE (each mirror a different manifest at one pair), GARBAGE, TRUNCATE, ENDLESS, SLOW, WITHHOLD_PROOF, WRONG_PROOF, MIXED_ROOT, FAST_FORWARD, APK_SWAP (a different core), APK_EXTRA (bytes added inside the block), DROP_MID_RANGE, EXPIRED_REPLAY (an old expired manifest with a higher pair than a long-offline client's), FLOOR_MAX (a stolen-key manifest with the floor at its maximum) and RELABEL (operator ids changed for known onions); a model log and witnesses with generated keys; a virtual clock with steps and per-device offsets within ±σ; a **recording installer port** that keeps every byte written to a session and fails the world if they differ from the checked bytes; crash enumeration at every SQL statement and every file operation of the §9.6.1 transactions (the F7 §8.3 method) with the structural invariants re-checked after each restart. **Named worlds:** the **stolen-release-key world** (a manifest signed by one stolen release key, served by one hostile mirror, lists only attacker mirrors and the floor at its bound; then RK4's root is published on the anchor mirrors only; every client must adopt it at its next update run, drop the stored manifest's authority and reset floor and conflict); the **recovery-epoch world** (two root keys stolen, attacker roots v(n+1) … v(n+5) logged; a recovery APK with a new chain is installed on half the clients, which must start a new epoch and follow only the new chain, while the other half stays on the old one); the **revocation world** and the **root-swap world** of revision 1.

**Twin worlds (T51).** Each pair differs in one input only and must give **identical mirror views** (every request's time, mirror, path, headers and range, over 60 virtual days), unless the column says otherwise:

| Twin | The two worlds differ in | Must not differ |
|---|---|---|
| NI-U1 | user activity: app opens, DM sends, channel reads, foreground time | **(a)** the update draws — which job indexes are update runs — over the same job sequence, whatever the activity; **(b)** with the same visibility intervals (activity differing only inside them), mirror views and the read schedule (T19). A job firing while the app is visible returns in both production and the world, so differing visibility legitimately moves which update runs happen, as it does for quiet runs (L10-D8) |
| NI-U2 | entitlement state: purchases, trials, an expired subscription, quiet-run issuer calls, **the payment hold** | mirror views (T2 untouched; update runs are not held) |
| NI-U3 | an offer and a pending download vs none (installed version current vs one behind; stored manifest fresh vs stale) | bundle requests (headers, paths); every update run's length; the timing of every later job (relay sessions, quiet runs) under the quota model; APK requests exist only where an offer exists, in fixed ranges |
| NI-U4 | stored root version (current vs three behind; the same anchor mirrors in all four roots) | bundle requests (paths, headers, mirrors); where roots change their anchor mirrors, the mirror choice differs by design (L10-D22) |
| NI-U5 | (release level) a wire-neutral release vs its predecessor | the wire transcript towards relays, the issuer and mirrors (G-D11): byte-identical preface, SETTINGS, WINDOW_UPDATE and request shapes |

### 11.3 Mutants (45; each detected against a same-scale control)

**Where they live.** The JVM worlds reach the Rust core only through the native host library, so a mutant of `ghost-client-update` cannot be "implemented only in test sources" (revision 1's claim). The Phase 9 pattern applies: the crate has a `mutants` feature (like `test-vectors` and `pq-spy` in `client-core/dm/Cargo.toml`) that compiles each mutation behind a switch; only `ghost-dm-host` enables it, exporting one test-only JNI symbol that selects a mutant per world; G-R6 allows that symbol only in libraries passed with `--test`, and a G-R2-style check proves the shipped graph lacks the feature. Kotlin mutants live in test sources.

| ID | Mutation | Killed by |
|---|---|---|
| MC-D1 / MC-D2 | root chain without the old / the new threshold | chain vectors; RK2 world |
| MC-D3 | manifest accepted under a key no longer in the root | revocation world |
| MC-D4 | duplicate signatures by one key counted twice | sigblock vectors |
| MC-D5 | network byte ignored | wrong-network vectors |
| MC-D6 | lower pair accepted | ROLLBACK mirror |
| MC-D7 | equal pair, other bytes accepted | EQUIVOCATE mirror |
| MC-D8 | expiry ignored | STALE world |
| MC-D9 | inclusion path not checked | WRONG_PROOF |
| MC-D10 | tree-head signature not checked | WRONG_PROOF variant |
| MC-D11 | witness quorum not enforced | quorum vectors |
| MC-D12 | cosignature by an unknown witness counted | quorum vectors |
| MC-D13 | response read past its bound | ENDLESS |
| MC-D14 | full-file hash skipped | APK_SWAP |
| MC-D15 | core or attestation check skipped | APK_SWAP, attestation vectors |
| MC-D16 | offer at or below the installed version | offer suite |
| MC-D17 | floor lowered by a manifest | trigger test, floor world |
| MC-D18 | older root adopted | root-rollback vectors |
| MC-D19 | one mirror per run | stale-mirror world |
| MC-D20 | proof of another manifest accepted | mix-and-match vectors |
| MC-D21 | block located from manifest data, not from the file | APK_EXTRA, crafted suite |
| MC-D22 | session committed without `apkFinish = OK` | recording installer |
| MC-D23 | a new root adopted without its log proof under the previous root's policy | the root-swap world (two stolen root keys, the attacker's own log and witnesses) |
| MC-D24 | both mirrors drawn from the manifest's list (no anchor mirror) | the stolen-release-key world |
| MC-D25 | floor not bounded by the manifest's attested releases, or not scoped to its root version | floor vectors; FLOOR_MAX; the stolen-release-key world |
| MC-D26 | core computed as "the file minus the block" (revision 1's rule, ignoring the padding) | crafted suite (real apksigner output, p > 0) |
| MC-D27 | signer read from the v3.0 block whatever the SDK | crafted suite (v3.1 lineage at SDK 33) |
| MC-D28 | trust anchor = the newer root by version (revision 1's rule) | the recovery-epoch world |
| MC-D29 | root leaf accepted only from a release key (revision 1's rule) | chain vectors (root-key leaf); the both-release-tokens-lost rotation case |
| MC-D30 | an expired manifest applies its directory and mirrors | EXPIRED_REPLAY |
| MC-D31 | a manifest overwrites a known onion's operator id | RELABEL |
| MC-D32 | the stored manifest stays current after a newer root | the revocation world |
| PM-D1 | check at app start | NI-U1 |
| PM-D2 | fetch on a namespace or issuer scope | scope test |
| PM-D3 | a header that differs between clients | request-builder test, NI-U3 |
| PM-D4 | conditional fetch with the stored hash | NI-U3 |
| PM-D5 | fetch of only the missing roots | NI-U4 |
| PM-D6 | update fetch inside a quiet run or relay session | `JobFate` and lease tests |
| PM-D7 | one rollout bucket across releases | bucket test |
| PM-D8 | APK ranges at resume offsets instead of fixed steps | NI-U3 |
| PM-D9 | the native fetch takes a path or range start supplied by Kotlin | JNI path-and-range test |
| PM-D10 | the native fetch accepts an onion outside the verified mirror set | JNI scope test |
| PM-D11 | an update run lasts until the download ends instead of `D_U` | NI-U3 (later jobs' timing under the quota model) |
| PM-D12 | update runs held by the payment hold, or decided after it | NI-U2 (hold axis) |
| PM-D13 | the bucket hashes the salt without the APK's SHA-256 | bucket test (a reused salt) |

The report line `update mutants: 45 run, 45 detected` is required by `update-report-check.sh` (G-D7).

### 11.4 Emulator and devices (V-D13, V-D14 become tests)

`update-emulator.yml` (**dispatch only, on a GitHub-hosted 2-vCPU Linux runner**, which has hardware-accelerated Android virtualization since 2024-04-02 per GitHub's changelog, within the minute budget of §12.4; the self-hosted runners lack KVM, so a nightly job there would be permanently red, as `dm-emulator.yml` is today; a recorded local run of the same script remains acceptable evidence, as in F9 §22.32 and F11 §17.1): API 29, 33 and 37 images; install v1 (fixture key), update to v2 through the engine against loopback model mirrors (a transport seam in the `gate` build type only, G-DM5's rules), user confirmation driven by UI Automator; a lower `versionCode` refused by the platform (`INSTALL_FAILED_VERSION_DOWNGRADE`); the rotated release (signed with `apksigner rotate` and `--lineage` by both fixture keys, G-D1's carve-out) installed over v2 on each image; then an old-key-only v4: refused on API 33 and 37, accepted on API 29 (the declared L10-D3).

**Live drill** (S9, manual, signed record; D26, Q37). A release build cannot be driven: it has no UI before Phase 13, no logging (`no-logging.sh`) and no exported trigger (T15m), and an instrumentation APK can drive an app only if it carries the target's signature, so instrumenting the RK1-signed build would need an instrumentation APK signed by the stagenet `K-APK` — one that could run code inside any stagenet install over adb, which Phase 16's testers will carry. The drill therefore has two halves on one real phone (API 33+; an API 29–32 phone if available):

1. **The updater half**: the `gate` build type of the stagenet configuration (the Phase 9 `dm-gate.sh` / `GateInstrumentation` pattern, signed with the gate key, package `org.ghost.app.gate`), embedding a **drill root** — a separate chain with the stagenet network byte, software keys on the drill workstation, listing the gate key's certificate in `apk_signers`, never in any release build (G-D4) — whose bundles the two real staging mirrors serve at `/ghost/v1/stagenet-drill/` and whose manifests and root are logged in the real Sigsum log (the monitor reports them as drills). `scripts/device/update-drill.sh` runs the gate instrumentation, which calls `Updates.requestCheck()`, `requestDownload()` and `install()`, confirms the platform dialog through UI Automator and writes `Updates.status` and every result code to the instrumentation output: a valid update installs; a tampered bundle, a rolled-back manifest, a manifest of another network, an APK with a byte changed and an old APK are refused, each with its exact code.
2. **The platform half**: the real RK1-signed stagenet releases installed with `adb install -r`: v(n) over v(n−1) accepted (the token-signed signature continues); a lower `versionCode` refused; after RK3, the rotated release (signed by both tokens) accepted and an APK signed by the old token alone refused on API 33+.

The signed record lists the phone model and API level, the build hashes, the bundle pairs served, each step's output and the log leaves. `K-APK` and the real stagenet root are exercised by RK1, `ghost-verify` on the real release, the platform half and the emulator; the updater's network, log and installer path by the updater half.

### 11.5 Drills (the gate's three drills)

| Drill | Case | Where | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tamper | each format byte-mutated (tight vectors refused, redundant material decision-neutral); APK swapped, extended, truncated, padding altered; proof of another manifest; manifest of another network; an expired manifest replayed | Rust, JVM, emulator, live (the updater half: tampered bundles served by a staging mirror at the drill path) | vectors, world report, emulator log, signed live record |
| Downgrade | lower pair, older root, release ≤ installed, below floor, fresh install of an old APK then first update run → `UNSUPPORTED`; a floor pushed to its bound by a stolen key, then reset by RK4's root | JVM, emulator, live (the platform half: a lower `versionCode` refused) | world report, emulator log, signed live record |
| Rotation | RK2 release key (also with both release tokens "lost": the root leaf signed by a root key); RK2 root key (two of three); RK2 witness set; RK2 attestation key; RK2 anchor-mirror change; RK3 Android key with lineage (two-token signing); RK4 emergency revocation and floor reaching a client whose manifest mirrors were all hostile; mirror retirement; a correctly signed but unlogged root refused (`ROOT_UNLOGGED`); a recovery APK starting a new epoch | JVM (formats, the stolen-release-key and recovery-epoch worlds), emulator (lineage), live with tokens on stagenet (RK2, RK3, RK5; the platform half for the lineage) | world report, emulator log, signed ceremony records |
| Rollout | 50 → 250 → 1000 permille with one salt; halt at 0; roll-forward of the previous source with the next counter; a wire-changing release refused at 50 by `manifest-build` | JVM, live (stagenet, after OA-11) | world report, live record |

### 11.6 Cross-environment reproducibility

`release-build.yml` green on the phase's closing tag: `SHA256SUMS(A) = SHA256SUMS(B)` for every path of §6.1, both builds from the committed recipe; the variation matrix of §6.6 recorded in Appendix C with each row's result.

### 11.7 The exit-gate argument

The four parts of §1.2 map to evidence: (1) tamper — T48's suites, the crafted-APK suite from real apksigner output, 32 security mutants, the emulator and both halves of the live drill; (2) downgrade — T49's suites, the platform's refusal on the emulator and the phone; (3) rotation — the RK2, RK3, RK5 ceremonies with real tokens on stagenet, the stolen-release-key and recovery-epoch worlds, and the emulator and phone lineage cases; (4) two environments — `release-build.yml` on the closing tag. T50 and T51 are not named by the plan's gate; they are this design's own guarantees (CS-D4, P-D1 … P-D5) and close with the world's twins NI-U1 … NI-U5 and the 13 privacy mutants. **What remains outside**: the auditor's attestation and its independent monitor runs, the production log policy, a second mirror operator, Google's platform behaviour, the project identity's legal holder, counsel (§1.2).

## 12. Gates, CI, dependencies

### 12.1 Gates (each with negative fixtures and a `self-test.sh` hit count)

| Gate | Rule | Supports |
|---|---|---|
| **G-D1** `release-no-secrets.sh` | (a) no signing material in the tree: PEM and OpenSSH private-key headers, Java and PKCS#12 keystores (by extension and magic), PKCS#8 files, onion secret keys anywhere under `infra/` (the search of `entitlement-schedule.sh`, extended), except listed test fixtures with throwaway keys (`protocol/test-vectors/apk/`, `test-harness/update/keys/`); (b) no workflow runs `apksigner`, `jarsigner`, `bundletool build-apks --ks`, a PKCS#11 tool, `ykman`, `yubico-piv-tool` or `sigsum-submit`, and no Gradle `signingConfig` other than the debug key of the smoke and gate builds (`app/build.gradle.kts:40,55`) — **with one carve-out**: `update-emulator.yml` may run `apksigner sign` and `apksigner rotate` only with `--ks` paths under `test-harness/update/keys/` (AGP's `signingConfig` cannot produce a lineage); self-test fixtures cover the carve-out (allowed), the same command with another key path and in another workflow (both refused); (c) G-L5's "nothing is published" stays | T52, TB-6 |
| **G-D2** `builder-pins.sh` | `infra/builder/pins.txt` is the single source: the Dockerfile's every `FROM` by digest, every download with a SHA-256 from it (the SDK, NDK and Rust archives included; no `sdkmanager` or `rustup` network resolution in the builder), the apt snapshot set; `ci.yml` (`RUST`, NDK, SDK packages, JDK), `rust-toolchain.toml`, `gradle-wrapper.properties`, `install-protoc.sh`, `build-native.sh` agree with it; no `target-cpu=native`, `-march=native` or `-mtune=native` in any build file; no `--cpus` in `build-release.sh` | T12, RQ-D3 |
| **G-D3** `release-sums.sh` | two `SHA256SUMS` are equal and complete (every path of §6.1, `ghost-verify` and the public export included); the APK's `lib/` entries equal the `lib/` lines; each symbol file's build-id equals its library's | T12, RQ-D18 |
| **G-D4** `release-records.sh` | per network: the committed root chain verifies by the chain rule from its first entry; the newest root lists the committed `apk-signer.crt` digest(s); the newest root's anchor mirrors equal `anchor-mirrors.txt` (≥ 2 operators on mainnet); the release counter never decreases over the first-parent history (the `entitlement-schedule.sh` history method); the tag-signing allowed-signers file exists; stagenet roots are never embedded in a mainnet build, and a drill root never in any release build | D2, D16, D26, RQ-D25 |
| **G-D5** = **G-R6 extended** (`dm-jni-surface.sh`) | `OTHER_JNI_CLASSES` gains `UpdateNative` and `TorMirrorTransport` with §9.3's closed lists, in the shipped library and in `ghost-dm-host` (`llvm-nm -D --defined-only`); the mutant switch only in `--test` libraries; only `android/update` and `android/network` reference `UpdateNative`; positive and negative fixtures for each | ADR-19, D20 |
| **G-D6** `update-rust-purity.sh` | `ghost-client-update` adds **exactly one** entry to the client graph and to `rust-dependency-allowlist.txt` (itself; `rust-client-allowlist.sh` otherwise unchanged); the `mutants` feature absent from every shipped graph (G-R2's method); its sources contain no `std::fs`, `std::net`, `std::time`, `SystemTime`, `Instant`, `println!`, `eprintln!`, `dbg!`, `log::` or `tracing::` (the G-R8 scanner, `test-harness/vendored-scan`, run on the crate) | T51, T3 |
| **G-D7** `update-report-check.sh` | the gate job's report has `update mutants: 45 run, 45 detected`, the NI-U1 … NI-U4 lines equal, the named worlds' lines, the drill lines; refuses a report claiming more than it measured (the `dm-report-check.sh` rule) | T48 … T51 |
| **G-D8** in `dependency-allowlist.sh` | `verification-metadata.xml` has no `trusted-artifacts` entry for `aapt2` (checksums instead) | D23 |
| **G-D9** `public-identity-scrub.sh` | every release output and every file bound for the public repository or the archive — APK assets and notices, SBOMs, the strings of the native libraries, symbol files and binaries, archive texts, the RK1 checklist, the public export — contains none of the patterns in `scripts/gates/identity-patterns.txt` (the owner's names, GitHub account, e-mail addresses, the private repository's URL, home and runner paths such as `/home/`, `C:\Users\`, `/home/runner/`); fixtures with each pattern; run by `build-release.sh` and by `static-gates` on the committed notices preamble and `Cargo.toml`'s `repository` field (both name the owner today, D25; S3 changes them to the provisional project identity in the same commit that adds the gate, Q45) | D25, RQ-D33 |
| **G-D10** `mirror-config.sh` | the committed mirror configuration: `access_log off`, `error_log` at `crit`, `log_not_found off`, `server_tokens off`, `etag off`, `if_modified_since off`, no `add_header` of a cookie or validator, both listeners present (§7.1); every compose service `logging: driver: none`; separate Tor instances for the serving and the upload onions; negative fixtures for each line | DS-13, DS-22 |
| **G-D11** `wire-transcript.sh` | the `rust` job regenerates the client's wire transcript (HTTP/2 preface, SETTINGS, WINDOW_UPDATE and every RPC's request shape towards relays, the issuer and mirrors, with fixed inputs) and it must equal `protocol/test-vectors/wire_transcript.txt`; the file's SHA-256 is artifact kind 24, from which `manifest-build` derives the wire flag (§7.6) | P-D5, NI-U5 |
| T15, T15m | `common.sh:15` gains `REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES` (ADR-51); `merged-manifest-lint.sh` lists `InstallStatusReceiver` | X-D8 |
| scopes | `no-logging.sh`, `anti-placeholder.sh`, `kotlin-clearnet.sh`, `sync-no-catch-all.sh` cover `android/update` and `client-core/update` | T3, T6, T8 |

| mingw-w64 (Debian package) | builder image, for the Windows `ghost-verify` | the mingw-w64 runtime's permissive terms, libgcc under the GCC Runtime Library Exception (what the linked binary carries is confirmed by V-D25) | ADR-50 |
| yubico-piv-tool and `ykcs11`, OpenSC | signer host only, never shipped | BSD-2-Clause; LGPL-2.1 (OpenSC, a separate program) | ADR-48 |

| nginx, or `ghost-mirror` | mirrors | BSD-2-Clause; proprietary | ADR-52 |
| diffoscope | CI diagnostic image only | GPL-3.0+ (a tool, never shipped or linked) | ADR-50 |
| Debian packages, Temurin 17, the Android SDK and NDK, the Rust toolchain | builder image (never distributed; each builder accepts the SDK licence) | various; Temurin GPLv2 + Classpath Exception | ADR-50 |
| **Not added** | `tough`, `aws-lc-rs`, any TUF library, any PKCS#11 Rust crate, any Gradle plugin (no CycloneDX Gradle plugin), OkHttp, any HTTP/1 client in the APK, any JSON parser in the client | — | — |

### 12.4 Hosted-runner minute budget (Q42)

GitHub Free gives a private repository 2 000 hosted minutes a month, blocked at exhaustion without a payment method (ADR-39 point 3); the self-hosted runners cost nothing (the announced fee was postponed, §6.4). Only two workflows use hosted minutes:

| Use | Minutes per run (V-D10 measures) | Runs per month | Minutes per month |
|---|---|---|---|
| `release-build.yml` job B | 60–120 (image from scratch, then the release on 2 vCPUs) | tags (≈ 2) + recipe or lock changes (≈ 2) + the monthly run (1) | 300–600 |
| `update-emulator.yml` | ≈ 90 (three API images) | dispatches (≈ 2) | ≈ 180 |
| **Steady state** | | | **≈ 480–780** |
| S0a and S3 iteration (one month) | 60–120 | ≈ 10–12 more B runs | + 600–1 440 |

The iteration month can approach the cap: S0a's B runs are spread over two months, B builds only the outputs under investigation while diagnosing (a dispatch input), and a mismatch is diagnosed in job C on the self-hosted runner from the cached outputs, not by re-running B. OA-5 confirms the budget before S0a; a payment method with a low spending limit is the owner's alternative (Q42).

| S5a | 0.5 | Android |
| S5 | 3.5 (the fourth job outcome, epochs, the gate instrumentation, the device-script check) | Android 2 |
| S6 | 3.0 (the quota job model, the named worlds) | QA, Android |
| S6b | 1.0 (+ ≈ 12 h of gate runs) | QA |
| S7 | 2.0 (`ghost-verify`'s platforms, the monitor's log-scanning client and served-window rule, the black-box checks) | part-time Rust, lead |
| S8 | 1.5 (the browser listener, G-D10, the no-log container test) | SRE |
| S9 | 2.0 (+ about 15 owner ceremonies: RK0, at least 3 RK1, 4 RK6 re-signings for the rollout, 4 RK2, RK3, RK4, RK5 — each with a Sigsum submission and a mirror upload; the drill harness) | QA, owner |
| S10 | 1.5 | lead |
| **Total** | **≈ 27 person-weeks** (revision 1: 20.5) | |

**Critical path**, recomputed (the review showed revision 1's 6–7 weeks did not follow from its own table). One Rust engineer carries S0a's share, S1 (2.5) and S4's Rust part (1.25): about 5.25 weeks to the end of S4. S5 (3.5 person-weeks for two Android engineers, ≈ 1.75 weeks) starts on S1's facade with a fake halfway through S1 and finishes its integration about half a week after S4: ≈ 5.75. S6 is built during S5; its net remainder (≈ 1) and S6b run in parallel: ≈ 6.75. S9 (≈ 2 calendar weeks, bounded by the owner's ceremonies) follows S7 and S8, which run beside S5 and S6: ≈ 8.75. S10 overlaps S9's second half: **≈ 9–10 calendar weeks** for the plan's team model, not the plan's 3–4 nor revision 1's 6–7 (X-D7, Q35). The part most likely to slip is S3: a cross-environment difference in R8 or Kotlin output has no known fix until it is seen (R-D1).

**The gate is bounded by owner actions, each with a lead time**, which is how Q35 presents it to the plan:

| Owner prerequisite | Needed before | Lead time |
|---|---|---|
| OA-5 hosted minutes confirmed (§12.4) | S0a | days |
| OA-1, OA-2 tokens and signer laptop | S0b, then S9 | 1–3 weeks of delivery |
| OA-4 domain and `_sigsum_v1` record | V-D11b (S0b) | days |
| OA-11 stagenet ES seq 2 (K1 ceremony, K2 publication) | the first real stagenet RK1 (S9); any stagenet release after 2026-10-26 | one ceremony and a relay/issuer update |
| OA-12 project identity (Q45): the provisional project name and accounts; the legal holder with counsel later | the first published release record (S9); the legal holder before Phase 16 onboards testers | days (provisional); counsel's calendar (legal holder) |
| OA-6 second staging mirror host | S8 | days |
| OA-13 drill phone(s) | S9 | days |

**Calibration on observed pace.** Phase 9's slices landed in about nine calendar days of parallel agent sessions against a 10–12-week estimate, while its gate evidence did not finish (F11 §17.2, STATUS:18). The same pattern is expected here: **implementation ≈ 30–40 agent-days (3–4 calendar weeks of parallel sessions)**, and the gate **waits on the owner actions above** and on gate-scale runs (S6b's re-baselines, the emulator dispatch), after which one to two weeks of drills and fix rounds.

### 13.3 Exit-gate checklist (all on the closing commit)

1. S0a's and S0b's V-D results recorded in Appendix C; no open fallback.
2. `rust`, `android`, `android-native`, `static-gates`, `reproducible` green **on a runner**.
3. `update-exit-gate`: the world at gate scale, `update mutants: 45 run, 45 detected`, NI-U1 … NI-U4 equal, the stolen-release-key and recovery-epoch worlds passed, crash enumeration; G-D7 passes; NI-U5 (G-D11) green.
4. `release-build.yml` green on the closing tag: A = B for every output of §6.1 (G-D3); G-D9 clean on those outputs.
5. `update-emulator.yml` green on API 29, 33, 37 on a hosted dispatch, or a signed local run of the same scripts recorded as such (tamper, downgrade, lineage).
6. RK0 (stagenet) done with the real tokens — the owner with the recorded checklist, and a witness if Q7 names one; the stagenet root (with its anchor mirrors) and certificate committed; G-D4 green.
7. Rotation drill on stagenet with the real tokens: RK2 (release key, including a root leaf signed by a root key; root key; witness set; attestation key; anchor mirror), RK3 (lineage, two-token signing), RK4 (revocation and floor), RK5 (restore from three shares); signed records.
8. Live drill, both halves (D26, §11.4): the updater half over the two staging mirrors and the real log refuses a tampered bundle, a rolled-back manifest and an old APK, and installs a valid update; the platform half shows signature continuity, the downgrade refusal and the lineage; signed record.
9. Rollout rehearsal on stagenet (after OA-11): 50 → 250 → 1000 with one salt, a halt, a roll-forward release, a wire-changing release refused at 50.
10. Appendix B applied (INVARIANTS T48–T52 and the changes; THREAT_MODEL; LIMITE L10 and the L4 amendment; plan errata; the ADR errata of B.5, F9's appendix copies included, under Q44; STATUS; ADR index); ADR-48 … ADR-52 decided or applied under the default rule.
11. This design review's findings (§18) and S10's code review corrected or recorded as owner decisions.
12. S6b's re-baselines recorded; the owner's monitor run over the stagenet log raises no alarm.
13. No UI claim (CP-10); STATUS says what ran where.
— **Release gates (not phase gates):** the auditor's attestation with `attest.t ≥ 1` on the release network; the mainnet RK0 and the production log policy; a second, independent mirror operator; the independent monitor's signed results (§5.3); the Android developer verification decision (Q18); the project identity (Q45) and the new public repository with `ghost-verify`; counsel's items (§16); the licence screen (Phase 13).

## 14. Risks

| ID | Risk | Likelihood / impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-D1 | A cross-environment difference (R8, Kotlin, AGP, file order, the host CPU or kernel) that pins cannot remove | medium / high (the gate) | the variation matrix first (S0a); the N = 2 processor pin and sources on tmpfs; diffoscope classes on cached B outputs; report upstream; never relax the gate to "equivalent" |
| R-D2 | The hosted runner (2 CPU, 8 GB, 14 GB) cannot hold the build | medium / medium | disk cleanup; V-D10 measures; fallback: a second owner machine that runs no CI (Q11) |
| R-D3 | Ed25519 signing on the YubiKey through PKCS#11 fails, or its message limit is lower than reported | low / low (Yubico's ykcs11 documentation lists `C_SignInit` with `CKM_EDDSA` and the Ed25519 key type, firmware 5.7+ required; docs.yubico.com, checked 2026-10-03) | short statements (≤ 60 B); `yubico-piv-tool`; fallback: encrypted software seeds on the air-gapped host |
| R-D4 | `--alignment-preserved` still changes bytes, or a later build-tools changes the padding rule, so `core(signed) ≠ unsigned` under §6.5's rule | low / high (revision 1's rule was already wrong: measured) | build-tools pinned at 37.0.0; V-D5 on real apksigner output with p = 0 and p > 0; the committed crafted APKs fail first on a change; fallback: zip-align in the build (part of the reproduced output) and sign preserved |
| R-D5 | No domain for the Sigsum rate-limit token (owner declines) | medium / medium | ask the log operators for another arrangement; else Rekor or a GHOST log with recruited witnesses (Q5), weaker |
| R-D6 | Android developer verification (2027) forces the advanced flow on users of an unregistered GHOST, or registration links the owner's identity | high / high | Q18 before Phase 16 onboards testers, with counsel; keep `K-APK` backed up; GrapheneOS and non-certified systems outside its stated scope |
| R-D7 | Accrescent stays closed, so GrapheneOS users have no store route | high / low | the mirror and `ghost-verify` work there; S-store kept ready |
| R-D8 | `K-APK` lost with its shares | low / critical | 3-of-5 SLIP-39 in five places; yearly RK5 |
| R-D9 | A missed heartbeat leaves every client STALE | medium / medium | heartbeat by day 77 (14 days of slack), at each K1 when earlier (calendar), 91-day window; Phase 13 warns at expiry only |
| R-D10 | Mainnet releases depend on the auditor's availability (`attest.t ≥ 1`) | medium / medium | two attesters in the root when possible; an RK2 that replaces an unavailable attester's key with another auditor's (logged); the threshold never goes below 1 on mainnet (§4.3) |
| R-D11 | Releases that change the schema or the ES cannot be rolled back | certain / medium | roll-forward; longer 5 % stage for such releases (Q30) |
| R-D12 | A bug in the custom formats or the APK parse (a CP-09 exception) | medium / high | vectors, independent JVM replay, sigsum-go vectors, crafted APKs, fuzzing, the Phase 15 audit |
| R-D13 | APK downloads over Tor are slow or break | medium / low | 4 MiB resumable ranges across update runs; user-initiated foreground download |
| R-D14 | Users skip `ghost-verify` for first installs | high / medium | declared (L10-D1); the fingerprint in two independent places (the public repository over Tor, the auditor's attestation) and on the mirrors' index page; Phase 13 copy |
| R-D15 | The witnesses of the default policy are not independent of each other | certain / medium | declared (L10-D6); adopt a more diverse named policy when Glasklar publishes one |
| R-D16 | A solo owner's ceremony load (heartbeats, releases, drills; after an RK3, two `K-APK` tokens at every release) | medium / medium | the 91-day window with a day-77 heartbeat; ceremonies scripted with expected outputs; the second party only for RK0, RK2, RK3; RK3 only on compromise (Q9) |
| R-D17 | Changing `applicationId` late costs every user a reinstall | low (if Q3 decided now) / high | Q3 before the first distribution |
| R-D18 | The hosted minutes (2 000 a month) run out during S0a and S3, blocking environment B | medium / medium | the budget of §12.4; path-filtered and monthly scheduling; diagnosis from cached outputs; a payment method with a low limit if the owner prefers (Q42) |
| R-D19 | Stagenet ES seq 2 is not signed in time, so `manifest-build` refuses every stagenet release after 2026-10-26 | high (today: not signed) / high (S9 blocks) | OA-11 as a dependency of S9 (§13.2's table); S2 on regtest |
| R-D20 | Update runs are rare for apps in low standby buckets, so revocations and floor raises arrive weeks late there | high / medium | declared (L10-D19); the user's own check; `manifestIssuedDay` shown by Phase 13; Q15 may raise the rate |
| R-D21 | Google's developer verifier looks GHOST installs and updates up under enforcement | high (from 2027) / medium | declared (L10-D24); V-D21 (a pre-auth token for a self-updater); Q18 with counsel |

| OA-9 | Engage the auditor for T12b (Phase 15) and agree the attestation format (with the expected signer digest), its publication and the independent monitor runs (§5.3) | the first mainnet release |
| OA-10 | Source escrow agent and public release conditions (ADR-38 point 10) | the first distribution |
| OA-11 | Sign stagenet ES seq 2 at a K1 ceremony and publish it by K2 to the issuer and the staging relays (Q38; `infra/issuer/RUNBOOK.md:254-256`) | S9's first real stagenet release (any stagenet release after 2026-10-26) |
| OA-12 | Decide the project identity (Q45): the provisional name, the project accounts (the new public repository, reachable over Tor, ADR-39 point 6), the notices' holder line; the legal holder with counsel | S9's first published release record; the legal holder before Phase 16 |
| OA-13 | A phone with Android 13+ for the live drill (an Android 10–12 phone too, if available) | S9 |

| ID | Legal or consulting item (counsel; engineering readings only here) |
|---|---|
| LG-1 | Android developer verification: registering (identity, package, certificate) versus not, and its terms (Q18) |
| LG-2 | Export-control classification of an encrypted messenger distributed from the EU (for example the mass-market treatment of encryption items in the EU dual-use regulation) — counsel's reading, none given here |
| LG-3 | The name "GHOST" and the `org.ghost` namespace against existing marks and projects (ghost.org); the final application id (Q3); the project identity and the copyright holder named in public material (Q45, D25) |
| LG-4 | Distribution terms: a licence for end users of a proprietary APK; terms for relay operators who receive `ghost-relay` (ADR-37 point 6); who the licensor is under the project identity (Q45) |
| LG-5 | Third-party notices and the MPL source offer on the mirrors (ADR-37 points 2, 10), with the public-history question already open |
| LG-6 | Auditor contract: publication rights for attestations and reports; escrow agreement |
| LG-7 | A privacy notice for the distribution channel (no logs at mirrors; what Google may receive on GMS devices, including the developer verifier's lookups) |
